If you actually need one of the fastest and best all-round PS5 SSDs, and you want one of the best capture cards that can capture and passthrough 4K 144 frames per second then go for it - but I really don't think that's necessary for anyone just buying this stuff for GTA 6. Case in point, the game will only run at 30fps, regardless of the platform you're playing on, and broadcasting in 4K60 is still the limit for streaming on Twitch and YouTube.

On a similar note, GTA 6's install size is monstrous, but let's assume for a minute that content creators who are being pitched this bundle do 90% of their gaming on a PC. They'll either have bought a brand new PS5 just for the luxury of playing GTA 6 at launch - and will thus have a clean drive with more than 210GB of space. Or, they'll have so few games on their machines anyway that an extra terabyte of internal storage won't really come in that handy anyway.

It's not as though having more internal space for clips and onboard recordings will help either. With an external capture card, everything will be stored on a PC, where more storage would actually be more helpful, probably with an external drive to save money. In recent years, PlayStation has made it more and more difficult to get your recorded gameplay clips off the PS5 and make them usable due to formatting anyway.

From a quick sift through what else is on offer, I'd argue content creators could do a lot better than Elgato's Twitch bundle, so I've created my own little bundle with more affordable products down below.

The 4KS is a much more affordable alternative right now, and it's still pretty feature-rich. You'll still get 4K60 passthrough and recording, HDR10 support, and access to all of Elgato's software bells and whistles.

Or, if you're really on a budget, the Neo will save you loads of money, allowing you to capture 1080p60 footage but play in full 4K60 - which, again, is actually more than you'll need for playing GTA6 at 30fps.

If you even need more storage space, an external HDD will help in either situation. The Seagate Portable HDD can easily store the inactive titles in your library. Alternatively, it'll let you transfer clips from a console to your PC, or just give you that backup room for more gameplay footage.

For more, take a look at the best controllers for GTA 6 and the best gaming headsets for GTA 6.

One of my earliest memories is playing SuperMario64 and wondering why the controller I held had three grips, but I only had two hands. Ever since I've been in love with video games and their technology. After graduating from Edinburgh Napier University with a degree in Journalism, I contributed to the Scottish Games Network and completed an Editorial Internship at Expert Reviews. Over the last decade, I’ve been managing my own YouTube channel about my love of games too. Now, I better stop myself here before I get talking about my favourite games like HUNT: Showdown, Dishonored, and Towerfall Ascension.

Quantum leap: IBM plans to scale beyond 1,000 qubits in 2027 with Nighthawk r3 processor

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

New unit sports more qubits and is planned to be part of future fault-tolerant rig

IBM has unveiled the Nighthawk r3, an upcoming next-gen quantum processor that will feature 520 qubits, as another step towards a planned fault-tolerant quantum system it hopes to launch in 2029.

Big Blue's current Nighthawk r2 was only made available in September and features 120 quantum bits (qubits) capable of handling circuits using 7,500 operations.

Its successor, IBM claims, will have more than four times as many qubits and execute 10,000 quantum operations to tackle larger, more complex problems while maintaining the same speed and accuracy.

Nighthawk r3 is slated to arrive in 2027, when Big Blue plans to link two of the quantum processors into a single system with more than 1,000 qubits.

IBM said applications increasingly require circuits executing thousands of quantum operations on approximately 100 qubits or more, speeding up collection of the millions or billions of samples needed to perform complex computations. Speed determines whether such demanding workloads can be completed in practical timeframes and at a reasonable cost.

The new quantum processor will feature 10 chip-to-chip interconnects, called l-couplers, designed for linking together processors that it will use to enable a single 1,040-qubit system. This tech was tested using Nighthawk r1 processors.

IBM previously outlined its goal of reaching reliable error correcting territory in 2029 with a quantum processor codenamed Starling. Now it intends for this to be the world’s first fault-tolerant quantum computer, capable of running quantum circuits comprising 100 million quantum operations on 200 logical qubits.

This is an ambitious goal, considering fault-tolerant quantum technology always seems to be at least a decade away, despite numerous breakthroughs and technology introductions. However, some in the technology industry believe the odds on a working system are shortening, given the increased activity around making encryption ready with support for quantum-resistant algorithms.

Then again, a professor at the University of Auckland New Zealand famously claimed last year that quantum computers hadn't so far managed to factor any number greater than 21 without cheating.

IBM presented a framework for Starling in a 2025 paper outlining hardware to implement the improbably named bivariate bicycle quantum low-density parity check (qLDPC) error correction codes. These significantly reduce the number of physical qubits needed to store and process quantum information, compared to existing surface codes.

Using the architecture in the paper, Big Blue intends to assemble Starling from modules that each contain a quantum memory and logical processing unit (LPU), allowing logic operations on the memory. The modules will have universal adapters letting them connect using l-couplers.

IBM is also preparing the way ahead by combining its quantum processors with classical high-performance compute (HPC) systems to research better quantum error correction.

Last week, Oak Ridge National Laboratory (ORNL) in the US announced such a system, combining an IBM Quantum System Two with Nighthawk r2 quantum processors and Umbra, powered by AMD 5th Gen Epyc CPUs and Instinct MI355X GPU accelerators.

IBM also has a partnership with Japan's RIKEN scientific research institute under which an IBM Quantum System Two has been integrated with the Fugaku Arm-based supercomputer, once the most powerful system in the world. ®

I Don’t Know How I Feel About ‘The Rachel’ Making A Comeback, But Jennifer Aniston Has Thoughts

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Since its debut in the mid-'90s, Friends has permeated various parts of pop culture and is still considered one of the best sitcoms of all time. Between the hilarious one-liners and memorable storylines, there is a lot to love. In fact, it made such an impact that women flocked to hair salons to get “The Rachel” haircut to mirror Jennifer Aniston’s beloved character. It seems like the ‘do is making a comeback, and while I don’t know how to feel about it, Aniston shared some thoughts.

Rachel Green’s iconic haircut, dubbed “The Rachel,” is a shoulder-length bouncy bob with layers. And even though it may seem like a lot, essentially every woman in the ‘90s wanted to have that same haircut. While the term "viral" wasn't really a thing at that point, that's now the best way to describe the craze. The haircut has now made its way to the 21st Century, as Gen Z has apparently found a love for it. Now, with a modern version of The Rachel making the rounds, with longer and softer layers, Aniston opened up to Elle about its return:

I love that people are still inspired by it. The modern versions feel a little more relaxed and lived-in, which I love.

I have mixed feelings on this. At this point, it feels kind of late for The Rachel to be going viral. It’s a ‘90s haircut, and it should stay in the ‘90s, even if there's a modern version. On the other hand, I do love that Aniston is all for the comeback and appreciates that the younger generation is jumping on the bandwagon. Of course, there have been plenty of ‘90s outfits and trends that have come back, so it probably shouldn't be too surprising that this 'do is one of them.

That being said, despite the look’s longevity for the past 30 years, Aniston was not exactly a fan of it back in the day. Rachel’s haircut may have seemed effortless and amazing, but the actress recalled that getting it together was easier said than done:

I remember how much work that original haircut was!

Considering how great Rachel’s hair looked throughout all seasons of Friends, I’m not surprised to know that it took some time to get it looking the way it did. Still, given the fact that The Rachel has been a consistent favorite over the years, it seems people don’t mind putting in the work to keep it up. It's funny to think about how nostalgia can influence different parts of our daily lives.

At present, what I wonder is whether 30 years from now, The Rachel will still be a hit. It’s hard to predict what fashion will look like in three decades or even in one, to be honest. However, if Friends continues to remain in the zeitgeist, which I’m sure will be the case, I wouldn’t be surprised if another version of the hairstyle comes along and fits with the times. For now, fans can see the look in all its glory by streaming Friends with an HBO Max subscription.

Natural Wonders: Crowley Lake Columns

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

It is low tide on Crowley Lake in Mono County, California. The water is still, the trout abundant, and one or two kayakers paddle about, enjoying the lack of tourists. But as you walk along the shore, something out of place appears: spiral pillars and arches that seem left over from some ancient civilization.

Crowley Lake is a man-made reservoir, administered by the Los Angeles Department of Water and Power. It was named after an important figure in the community, affectionately known as the Desert Padre. This beloved Catholic priest worked tirelessly to help a community that experienced water shortages throughout the 1920s and 1930s. Water from the nearby Owen Valley was diverted to Los Angeles, leaving very little for local residents.

Father Crowley made it his mission to bolster tourism in the area. The publicity led authorities to help locals by drawing up plans for a dam and reservoir. When Father Crowley died in a car accident, authorities completed the Long Valley Dam and named the reservoir Lake Crowley. If it weren’t for his intervention, the lake wouldn’t have existed.

As the years passed, visitors continued coming to Lake Crowley to fish, kayak, camp, and hike. Then something weird started to appear on the cliffsides. The banks appeared to be receding and hollowing out, revealing hundreds upon hundreds of spiral columns. Many onlookers believed they were man-made. Some compared the formations to Moorish architecture from North Africa and Spain.

Though the reservoir is man-made, the area’s history dates back millennia. Crowley Lake lies in the wider Long Valley Caldera, one of Earth’s largest calderas.

The caldera sits atop one of three ancient supervolcanoes in the United States. Around 760,000 years ago, this supervolcano erupted and spewed 650 cubic kilometers of rhyolite rock and ash into the air for a week. It covered the land in a blanket of ash and debris over one kilometer thick. And it left behind a 32km long, 18km wide, and 900m deep depression in the earth.

The depression formed when the subterranean magma chamber emptied onto the surface and the remnant shell of a roof collapsed. The blast and pyroclastic flow were so powerful that ash fell as far as Kansas. Along the rim are the lava dome complexes of Mammoth, Glass, and Bald Mountains. The caldera also hosts a cluster of hydrothermally active lakes called the Mammoth Lakes.

The main feature left by the eruption is the Bishop Tuff, a few square kilometers in area. A tuff is a rock of compacted volcanic ash. At the base of this tuff stand the Crowley Lake Columns. They range in thickness, average about six meters high, and are generally reddish, beige, grey, or white with a dark water line at their base. Some lean while others stand straight.

Attached by arches, the columns are so evenly spaced that to some visitors, it resembles an ancient temple. Horizontal cracks about 30cm apart add further detail to the columns. They look like they hold up the cliffs but they simply remained after the softer cliff rock started to erode.

The high levels of silica, sodium, and potassium in the columns make the rock conduct heat well and cool suddenly. This led to the cracks. Similar formations exist in Guatemala, Mexico, and New Mexico.

The few people who frequent Crowley Lake have long known about the columns. Only recently, social media has started to highlight them. This is due to its seclusion on the lake’s shoreline. The only ways to access them are on foot or by boat, kayak, or 4×4. The dirt road on which you turn from Highway 395 is so steep and uneven that it can bog down vehicle. Visitors tend to drive halfway, then walk.

Despite popular speculation that these columns are an example of columnar jointing, they are not. The geological process is different. With columnar jointing, the spaces are not so far apart. A study by geologists at UC Berkeley used a combination of computer models, X-ray diffraction, and electron microscope imagery to determine how they formed. It turns out that the culprit is simply…water.

“We hypothesize that the columns are a result of instabilities at the liquid water and steam interface as cold water seeped into the still-cooling Bishop Tuff,” says Noah Randolph-Flagg, the study’s lead scientist.

The columns formed through hydrothermal convection. Photo: Shutterstock

Essentially, rainwater seeped down into the hot ash and started to boil, producing a mineral glue called mordonite. The boiling water formed a column of steam which carried the mordonite upward through the rock. Thus, the mineral hardened the rock, leading it to remain standing while the surrounding rock eroded. This process is called hydrothermal convection or circulation.

According to Randolph-Flagg, it only took 100 years to form these columns. The study also states that “the liquid water–steam interface probably is the source of the instability that generates columnar flow.”

As for the erosion of the cliffside, the reservoir’s water levels fluctuate throughout the year with the seasons. After the completion of the reservoir, the columns started to reveal themselves. The constant rise and fall as well as a repeating freeze-thaw cycle caused the tuff to recede. It revealed the columns and also hollowed out the cliffside to form shallow caves. Take care when going into these little caves or pushing on the columns, as they might not be as stable. As the cliff continues to erode, we should see more of these columns.

The arches resemble Moorish architecture. Photo: Shutterstock

In terms of the Long Valley Caldera, scientists have closely monitored its activity, which has been increasing since the 1980s. Since it is a one of only 20 supervolcanoes in the world, they have paid close attention to its potential for eruption, despite its status as inactive.

There have been small earthquakes and signs of movement in the earth as if it were “breathing.” This is seen in Yellowstone, another supervolcano. While there are no immediate signs of an impending eruption, the caldera’s history and current activity suggest that it is possible.

Kristine De Abreu is a writer at ExplorersWeb.

Kristine has been writing about Science, Mysteries and History for 4+ years. Prior to that, Kristine studied at the University of Leicester in the UK.

Based in Port-of-Spain, Kristine is also a literature teacher, avid reader, hiker, occasional photographer, an animal lover and shameless ramen addict.

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Crystal Palace have sacked Pierre Sage just 16 games into his tenure.

Sage, who led Lens to a surprise second-place finish in Ligue 1 last season, was appointed in the summer as Oliver Glasner's replacement, after he left for Nottingham Forest.

However, Sage has made a poor start, with Palace languishing at the bottom of the Premier League after 10 games.

Palace have also won just one of their four Europa League games, though they have reached the quarter-finals of the EFL Cup.

Sage won five games in competitions, drawing three, but Palace have shipped 30 goals. Among teams from Europe's top five leagues, only his former club Lens (31) have conceded more goals this campaign.

One of the other clubs tied with Palace on 30 goals conceded, Manchester United, also sacked their head coach on Thursday, with Michael Carrick's departure likely paving the way for Mauricio Pochettino to take the reins at Old Trafford.

Palace's 29.6 xGA in all competitions is the highest total of any team in Europe’s top 5 leagues.

The Eagles rank as the worst team in the Premier League this term for goals conceded (24), xG against (21.9), shots faced (189), goals conceded via crosses (seven), goals conceded via headers (seven) and the percentage of minutes trailing (54.4%).

Crypto hack losses equal 57 cents per $1 earned onchain: Ark’s Valente

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Crypto hackers stole $8.7 billion so far in 2026, surpassing the projected annual onchain revenue generated across the industry, according to ARK’s research director.

Hackers stole 57 cents for every $1 earned onchain by blockchain protocols since 2017, according to Lorenzo Valente, director of digital asset research at ARK Investment Management.

“Security is the bottleneck,” wrote Valente in a Thursday X post, sharing a chart showing that $8.7 billion lost to crypto hacks year to date had already surpassed the $5.6 billion in estimated annual onchain revenue for 2026.

Since 2017, the crypto industry has lost about $26.9 billion to hacks and earned $47 billion in total onchain revenue, he added.

The recent $6.3 billion stolen in the Binance hack alone exceeded Valente’s estimate for full-year 2026 onchain revenue. Value lost to hacks is on track to exceed onchain revenue by year-end for the first time since 2020.

The crypto industry saw a string of high-value hacks this year, including September’s $387 million Bitget hack and the $320 million Liquid Network exploit, as well as April’s $295 million Drift Protocol exploit.

The mounting security exploits are weighing on the institutional appetite for DeFi. In an April research note, JPMorgan analysts identified bridge security as a long-standing challenge for the industry.

Total value locked in decentralized finance (DeFi) has fallen nearly 10% since the Binance exploit, from $85.9 billion on Oct. 29 to $77.4 billion on Thursday, according to DefiLlama.

What the Mythology Behind Tree Pose Reveals About Unlocking Your Inner Strength

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

(Photo: Andrew Clark; Design in Canva by Laura Harold)

Yoga Journal’s archives series is a curated collection of articles originally published in past issues beginning in 1975. This article about Tree Pose mythology first appeared in the January-February 1989 issue of Yoga Journal.

According to Joseph Campbell, the symbol of the tree in Indian mythology is deeply rooted in the pre-Aryan civilization of the Indus Valley, which flourished around 2000 B.C. In particular, one of the iconographic seals excavated earlier this century from the ruined city of Harappa shows a nude goddess standing in the branches of a sacred pipal tree, surrounded by female attendants and other supplicants engaged in a fertility ritual or sacrifice.

The motif of a tree-goddess sheltered in the boughs of a tree reappears in all subsequent periods of Indian art, whether Buddhist, Hindu, or Jain. Buddha himself attained enlightenment under a pipal tree, also known as the bodhi tree, or Tree of Wisdom, and early Buddhists shared with the more primitive fertility cult the popular belief that trees are divine beings with magical properties. Hence, at the early Buddhist stupas at Sanchi, figures of female tree guardians (yakshas) are given special prominence on the ornamental gateways leading to the egg-shaped monument.

The belief in the magical powers of trees and tree-spirits lingers on even today in rural India, as shown by the following anecdote from the early life of the Indian saint Nityananda. “He sometimes wore a loincloth, sometimes was nude, and lived a life of great simplicity;” writes biographer M. U. Hatengdi. “His home was in the rocks, caves, and forests. It was a familiar sight to see him perched on some tree top, standing stiff…”

“Crowds often gathered below the tree on which he stood, crowds that were mixed without regard for caste or creed. After a time, the Master would shower them with leaves: these leaves were greatly prized as medicine. One day a man who had lost his sight stood among the crowd beneath the tree…He continued pleading with the Master to restore his sight, explaining that as it was, he was not able to earn a living and so was a burden to his family. After a considerable time, Nityananda came to him and rubbed the man’s eves with leaves from the tree…On arising the next morning, the man was able to see and his sight remained restored.”

Nityananda was a saint who practiced the most severe of yogic austerities. During his early years, he ate food only when it was offered to him by others. He wore a loincloth not from modesty but at the insistence of his disciples. “He had a total disregard for the elements or for his nightly resting place…and would sit or lie down any place, from the doormat and dunghill to the cattleshed and even the dry lavatories, just to avoid crowds.” He believed that vairagya (total renunciation or absence of desire) was essential for his spiritual progress.

Vrksasana (Tree Pose) itself derives from a traditional act of austerity. According to scholar Georg Feuerstein, the practice of standing on one leg (known as Eka Pada Sthana) has been followed in India for at least two thousand years, and is referred to in the Mahabharata. In that great Indian epic, Pandu stands on one leg all day long in order to propitiate the god Indra (1.123), and Manu (the father of the human race) stands on one leg for a thousand years (III.187).

The most beautiful and dramatic representation of the practice of Eka Pada Sthana is found in the huge relief-sculpture at Mamallapuram, near Madras, dating from the seventh century A.D. Here, as Heinrich Zimmer writes, “A huge wall of rock, rising vertically in the fierce sunshine of southern India, has been converted into a prodigious masterpiece, teeming with hosts of gods, titans, genii, serpent princes, human beings, and animals, all converging towards a great cleft in the middle of the composition,” which represents the descent of the Ganges from heaven to earth.

This ancient Shaivite myth relates how the holy sage Bhagiratha performed great acts of austerity for a thousand years to compel the gods to let the heavenly river Ganges flow down to Earth, and how he then persuaded Lord Shiva to receive the full weight of the cataract on his head, lest the Earth be destroyed by the impact of the falling water. At Mamallapuram, we see the stoic figure of Bhagiratha standing on one leg, with his arms raised overhead in supplication, at the very moment when Shiva gives his consent and all the orders of creation burst into celebration at the flow of the sacred river.

The Descent of the Ganges as depicted at Mamallapuram can be interpreted as an allegory of our spiritual journey. Through clarity of purpose and unwavering dedication, the solitary yogi reaches a place of inner stillness—as quiet as the rock, as empty as the void.

But in that emptiness, like the emptiness that existed before creation, there is potential for a tremendous surge of psychic energy (the Ganges). This energy can overwhelm or corrupt the individual, as all power tends to corrupt, unless it is offered back to its divine source (Lord Shiva) and received as a sign of grace, not as confirmation of an inflated ego. When we surrender in this way, we begin to experience the vibratory movement of the world, and we know at last the joy of creation.

The Expanse: Osiris Reborn Shows Off Promising Gameplay in New Trailer

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Visit an abandoned asteroid mine with the crew in The Expanse: Osiris Reborn latest trailer

The 44th annual Golden Joystick Awards opened the 2026 video game awards season. Beyond celebrating and rewarding the year's best games, the ceremony also featured exciting announcements and reveals. Owlcat Games shared an in-depth look at its upcoming sci-fi action RPG, The Expanse: Osiris Reborn, with the premiere of a new gameplay trailer. The 3-minute video unveiled interesting details about the 24th-century setting, gameplay mechanics, and exploration sites.

The Expanse is a novel series written under the pen name James S. A. Corey, used by co-authors Daniel Abraham and Ty Franck. It has been adapted for television into a critically-acclaimed series, and it will now return in the form of a video game called The Expanse: Osiris Reborn. Sci-fi enthusiasts are always on the lookout for a new space-faring game, so with a Spring 2027 release, this might just be players' next pick.

The "Becoming a Captain" trailer showcases a mission where the protagonist has to rescue a group of rebellious teens who have escaped to an abandoned asteroid mine riddled with space pirates. Players must speak to a specific NPC at a bar and accept his request to initiate this mission. It is entirely optional but extremely rewarding; completing the mission and bonding with one of the teens, Polly, might get her to join the player's crew later on. This will give players access to her special ability, which spawns a swarm of nanobots — a feature we see in the trailer.

Besides companion abilities, the players will have access to an array of other skills. In the trailer, we see them activate a spatial detection device, throw a small explosive projectile directly at the chest of a pirate, and launch a lethal device at an enemy behind cover. Gameplay isn't only about fun gadgets, though. The Expanse series is known for trying to avoid bending the rules of science. Osiris Reborn appears to be taking the same approach, as we can see from the lack of exterior sound during space fights on the asteroid. You can also notice the protagonist jump from one surface to another and easily stick to them in zero gravity, presumably wearing magnetic boots.

Fans of the franchise are happy to see the return of the series, even if it is through a completely different medium. However, Osiris Reborn isn't exclusive to fans of this universe; whether you're a sci-fi fan, a video game enthusiast looking for a fun RPG, or a heartbroken Mass Effect fan looking for your next story-driven space odyssey, this might just be the right game for you.

Poulet Basquaise

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Rich tomatoes, stewed onions and peppers, and tender bone-in chicken make this Spanish- and French-inspired poulet Basquaise recipe extremely flavorful while still being surprisingly easy to make. Savory and rustic, it’s a stew that’s ideal for making ahead and enjoying all week long. The key ingredient? Piment d’Espelette—the regional spice known for its peppery, smoky essence. If you’re looking to master a classic dish or simply want a reliable, cozy weeknight dinner, then this recipe won’t let you down.

Heat a large skillet with oil and season the chicken with salt. Working in batches, add the chicken pieces and sear until the skin is golden in color, which should take 5 to 7 minutes. Remove the chicken to a heatproof plate.

Then reduce the heat and add the onion, bell peppers, garlic, piment d’Espelette, and salt. You’ll want to stir this once in a while so it doesn’t stick, but also let the vegetables soften. This should take 8 to 10 minutes.

At this point, increase the heat again and add the wine. Scrape up any bits on the bottom and allow those peppers and onions to absorb the wine. The liquid should reduce.

Add the tomatoes and broth and bring to a simmer. Be careful of splattering sauce and adjust the heat as needed. Place the chicken back into the skillet, skin side up, and cook until the chicken is tender and nearly falling off the bone. The sauce should also be thicker and fragrant. This should take about 30 to 40 minutes.

Stir in the chorizo and cook until it’s warmed through. Top it off with thyme and serve!

The full list of ingredients and instructions can be found in the recipe below.

Let cool and store in an airtight container in the refrigerator for up to 5 days. It’s certainly a great meal-prep dish. Make this on a Sunday night for lunch Monday and Tuesday.

Hanifa Is Back: Inside the Return of the Black-Owned Luxury Fashion Brand After an Eight-Month Pause

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

After eight months away, Anifa Mvuemba reflects on the decision to pause and shares what's ahead.

“Is that Hanifa?” is a question I got often walking down any street, from Miami, New York, to D.C. and Los Angeles. The clothing brand built a cult following over the years, largely made of career-focused, fashion-obsessed Black women around the world who loved existing as a “Hanifa woman.” Anifa Mvuemba originally launched the label in 2012, initially gaining traction after she posted a self-made 21st-birthday dress on Instagram. The brand slowly continued to grow, and then eventually blew up on a widespread global stage during the COVID-19 pandemic when it launched its groundbreaking “Pink Label Congo” collection via a viral 3D virtual runway using headless digital avatars.

The independent brand became synonymous with luxury, sensuality, and well-made designs. But that all came to a halt this past March when Mvuemba announced a pause for the brand due to an assortment of backend issues—from shipping delays, warehouse complications, and burnout.

But after taking time for herself and resetting, Mvuemba is entering a new chapter. And Hanifa is set to return November 18, 2026. We caught up with the 35-year-old founder to talk about her time away and what shoppers can expect now that Hanifa is coming back.

Honestly, Hanifa is like a part of my DNA at this point. I started this brand when I was 21, and so I think I just needed a moment to just take a breather, rest, be with my new baby, enjoy my family, enjoy this time, and just think about how I wanted to move forward. It was a lot, but eventually I was just like, ‘Okay, I need to get back to work,’ because I genuinely enjoy and love what I do, creating. I'm such a creative person, and I love business. I can't leave a Hanifa alone like that. I can't do it.

I really was thinking about the opportunity that this pause awarded us. It gave us the opportunity to build this dream world of Hanifa that I've always wanted to have. To be completely honest, within the last couple of years, I always wanted to have a moment to fix things, rebrand a few things, and I felt like I finally got the opportunity to be able to do that without feeling the pressure of doing all of that. So we were able to go back into our archives. I really wanted to start there, and think about the woman that we are building for. Just looking at those moments that the customers fell in love with from the beginning, and reintroducing that in different ways. Of course, keeping the woman, her body, at the center. I wanted for this new era for the brand in design to feel a little bit more elevated, a little refined, while still remaining true to Hanifa. And then just exploring different things. We really wanted to try different materials. Knitwear being our biggest category, wanting to explore different ways that we can do knitwear, color, textures, fits, patterns, while still staying true to the brand and what people love us for.

I really understood the importance of resting. I'm someone that enjoys working, but I think rest is actually necessary to be able to have peace of mind. I thought it was necessary for me to be present for my family, also for me to be the best leader that I could be for my team, and I think it's okay to rest. I'm grateful that we were able to even pause for this long. It's not usually the norm for independent brands. But I'm grateful that we even had that opportunity to be able to say, “Hey, we're gonna step away for eight months, 10 months, and just figure it out and do all the things that we needed to do.”

I'm excited for people to experience this new era of Hanifa. We've been working so hard, considering every touchpoint for the customer, restructuring the business. I mean, we've literally been uprooting everything, and refining, going back and forth and redoing the website. I'm very, very excited for everyone to really experience this world that we're building. And so the collection is called World of Anifa. We're now allowing them into this space and making the brand feel more full. We always speak about how we've grown with our customers, we're journeying with our customers, and wanting them to experience what it is to be in this world, and how we actually consider them in the decisions we make too.

She's evolving. I think we're all evolving. I was 21 when I started, my perspective was different. Now I'm turning 36 this year, and everything is shifting. So she's grown with us. We've grown with her. She's rediscovering herself. She's maybe a new mom. Maybe she just got her new executive position. Maybe she just started a business. She's in a new season in her life, and so she still wants to feel seen. She still wants to look good. Also a big thing that we're building is the categories, really diversifying that so that she can really have Hanifa all the time. Kid drop-offs, airport outfit. You know, we're thinking about every single detail.

I don't care. Period. But seriously, it took a while for me to get to this space. I do care actually because I see it affecting my peers and people that I care about, too. When I announced the pause, I received so many DMs, messages, and support from other women who own businesses, Black women, too, who shared what they've experienced, similar situations of what we went through. And I realized that there really isn't a safe space for us in this world, and so that's also something that I'm working on on the back end, figuring out how we can have these safe spaces for founders like me, where we can lean on each other, support each other, because I think we'll just be stronger as a unit. Literally, a friend of mine reached out to me recently. Was just like, “Oh my god, this is going on.” I'm just like, “Here's what to do. Here's what you could do.” Being able to support each other in that way, I think that's the most important thing because you really can't focus on the noise. I think that was the hardest thing: just type it, delete it. Respond and delete your response. But at the end of the day, the algorithm makes you feel like everyone is talking about it when in reality they're not. So you just kind of have to take it for what it is and move on.

I think in this new era, I really want to focus on educating the customer and just letting them know what it is, and figuring out the best way that we can talk about what goes into building these brands that we have and building this world and letting them in, giving them a little bit more of that insight, so that there's more of an understanding. Because even with the chatter that happened earlier this year, as I was just processing and reflecting and reviewing, I was like, “There's also a lot of like misinformation,” and I'm like, “I don't even know if they really even understand.” We want to make sure we have an educational component within that so that we can make sure that that's clear because the girls need to know.

Aiyana Ishmael is the style editor at Cosmopolitan magazine. In her work, Aiyana focuses on the culture of fashion and how it intertwines and shapes the zeitgeist. She is an award-winning journalist from Miami, Florida, and a graduate of the historically Black university, Florida A&M. She is a 2024 Forbes 30 Under 30: Media honoree, and her debut romance novella PASSING GAME is set to release March of 2027 (831 Stories/Simon & Schuster).

Are Alt-L1s over? Security, costs drive smaller chains to Ethereum

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

L1 blockchains Zilliqa, Gnosis and Ronin join the Ethereum ecosystem as the costs of going it alone grow, and the security and liquidity advantages become too hard to resist.

Running an independent blockchain has always come with a particular promise: control.

A standalone Layer 1 architecture allows projects to be the masters of their own destiny, set the rules of consensus, maintain their own validators and build infrastructure around their users, without relying on any other network.

But with great power comes great responsibility — and considerable expense.

Blockchains need enough users, developers and economic activity to support the infrastructure that secures them. For some smaller networks, keeping an entire ecosystem afloat no longer makes economic sense.

Zilliqa is the latest project to confront that reality, announcing plans last week to move away from its L1 architecture and become an Ethereum-based Layer 2 (L2) instead:

“Usage never reached what a standalone chain needs,” the post explained. “Funding and liquidity for standalone L1s dried up. The recent hacks showed how much it takes to secure a chain alone. The data is onchain for everyone to see.”

Blockchain ecosystem Gnosis (which has always been Ethereum-adjacent) is also working to transition Gnosis Chain into an Ethereum-based network through its Ethereum Economic Zone (EEZ) initiative. Its RollupX test network intends to show how participating chains can share Ethereum settlement and synchronous composability as native rollups.

Gaming-focused Ronin blockchain also completed its move back to Ethereum as an L2 in May.

With interest in alt L1s drying up along with the funding, will we start to see more and more chains consolidating in the Ethereum ecosystem?

Independent L1s generally have to keep several core functions going, including execution, consensus, data availability and settlement. They must also fund the token incentives used to secure their networks through staking.

Related: What are native rollups? Full guide to Ethereum’s latest innovation

“It comes down to a chain’s profitability,” Max Shannon, senior research analyst at Bitwise, tells Magazine.

ZIL becomes a Layer 2 on Ethereum. Source: Zilliqa

“L2s only need to maintain execution and do not necessarily need a token, which makes them a more attractive financial and operational proposition,” he says.

Shannon says the top ten largest projects are more financially sustainable. Coinbase and Robinhood’s L2 networks generated $48 million and $52 million in fees respectively over the last 365 days, with margins above 90%. The next eight largest chains each generated more than $1 million annually.

From 11th place onward, however, annual fees collectively amounted to less than $1 million. So a network may have ambitious technology and a dedicated community, but those things don’t necessarily pay the bills.

Ethereum is often criticized for giving away too much of its revenue to L2s, but that’s precisely what makes it attractive to set up projects there.

But just becoming an Ethereum L2 doesn’t automatically make a network profitable however. Shannon points to Optimism, Katana and Linea as examples of networks whose expenses outweigh their fee revenue. Projects like Blast have already been forced to shut down.

Gnosis has targeted spring 2027 to complete its transition, which will see it give up its independent validator-based consensus in favor of Ethereum settlement.

Gnosis Chain will relaunch as an Ethereum Economic Zone. Source: Gnosis

“Two years ago I gave a talk about ‘native Rollups’ that would truly extend Ethereum instead of being isolated chains,” co-founder Martin Köppelmann said, adding that RollupX “even goes beyond what I had imagined back then!”

Gnosis’s EEZ initiative aims to enable separate Ethereum-based networks interact synchronously, so a smart contract on one network can call another and use its response within the same transaction, without relying on the usual bridge-and-wait process.

That’s a prospect that has some Ethereum supporters particularly bullish, with ETH bull materkel describing the EEZ as a potential “black hole for liquidity,” and saying it could allow participating networks to tap into Ethereum’s total value locked (TVL) and strengthen its position as a liquidity hub.

Could network effects, and increased interoperability draw more and more chains into Ethereum’s orbit?

It isn’t only the operating cost economics that make sense. Projects can also tap into an established ecosystem of applications and developers rather than building those network effects from scratch.

Related: Privacy tools are rising behind institutional adoption, says ZKsync dev

Alex Gluchowski, co-founder and chief executive of Matter Labs, the company behind Ethereum L2 ZKsync, says he expects more L1 teams to maintain their own chains while using Ethereum for settlement.

“Running an independent L1 means maintaining its security as well as building something people want to use,” Gluchowski tells Magazine.

But Gluchowski says sovereignty and Ethereum integration are not mutually exclusive, since chains can retain control over their rules and operations while using Ethereum for shared settlement.

Ronin completed migration to Ethereum in May. Source: Ronin

And for applications handling sensitive financial information, maintaining a private environment under their own control can be essential, he says. Shared settlement does not have to mean exposing confidential transaction data.

The biggest obstacle today is that all the L2s are effectively isolated from each other and from Ethereum. It’s certainly possible to jump between them, but its far from seamless and interoperability is poor.

“Fragmentation is already happening,” Gluchowski says, pointing to the difficulty of connecting networks when transactions involve privacy.Interoperability between private environments will become more important as more applications seek to combine confidential activity with access to public blockchain liquidity, he says.

Shannon cautions against describing the market as undergoing straightforward consolidation around Ethereum.

Some large institutions are launching L2 networks, he says, including Coinbase and Robinhood, but others are still choosing to build independent L1s, like Circle’s Arc, Stripe’s Tempo, or Hyperliquid’s purpose-specific perpetual futures blockchain.

“So there is no clear conclusion to the L1 versus L2 debate as yet,” he says.

Media Briefing: The IAB finds AI measurement standards elusive for publishers amid platform data gaps

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

This Media Briefing covers the latest in media trends for Digiday+ members and is distributed over email every Thursday at 10 a.m. ET. More from the series →

This week’s Media Briefing looks at how the IAB’s months-long effort to standardize AI attribution ran into a roadblock: the lack of visibility into what happens inside AI platforms.

The Interactive Advertising Bureau spent months trying to determine how the industry should attribute and credit conversions influenced by AI. But it ran into a problem: you can’t standardize what you can’t see.

Digiday first reported in August that the IAB was developing an AI attribution framework to standardize measuring and crediting AI’s role in conversions, and the signals to quantify AI’s influence on purchase decisions. The IAB’s working group included tech companies, publishers, agencies, measurement vendors and brands — including People Inc., Walmart, WPP Media, Emberos, Criteo, Bluefish, Microsoft, Brandlight, Tinuiti and Prudential Financial. The resulting “AI-Era Attribution Blueprint,” was published today.

The blueprint addresses some of the questions behind the effort, but it also underscores how much remains unmeasurable. Recommendations, consumer decisions and even transactions may happen inside AI platforms, but that data isn’t yet accessible to advertisers or measurement providers.

“Going in, I thought that we were going to help figure out how credit was assigned through the process. I realized actually what this document was starting to become was more of a call to action to the industry,” Caroline Giegerich, the IAB’s vp of AI said during a virtual IAB roundtable on Monday. “There are evidence layers that are missing. We’ve started to have conversations on that, but there’s still room to go. But without remarking on what’s missing, what we want to see, and AI’s influence in the purchase funnel, I don’t think we’re going to get there. So it kind of started in one place, and over the course of four months, ended in another.”

As Digiday previously reported, the measurement gap has prompted a growing number of startups to pursue solutions.

The IAB’s blueprint offers a framework for understanding AI’s role in driving business outcomes, while acknowledging that data gaps make its influence difficult to reliably measure and attribute. It also underscores a broader challenge for the industry: as publishers expand their GEO offerings to brands, measurement remains a major obstacle.

But publishers are experimenting with ways to address parts of the measurement gap. Time is working with Mobian to measure the performance of the ads it serves to AI agents, for example, and Bustle Digital Group is working with Profound to measure its GEO campaign performance for brands. Those efforts offer visibility into specific activity, but they don’t yet solve the broader challenges of tying AI’s influence to sales.

AI search makes it more difficult for brands to measure how LLMs influence consumer purchases, because impressions, clicks and referral data aren’t always available when an AI assistant guides consumers from product discovery to purchase decisions. And even when an AI recommendation does lead to a sale, those conversions can be incorrectly attributed to search, direct traffic, or other channels, according to the IAB’s report.

That attribution gap can distort marketing investment decisions, with spend going to channels that capture conversions rather than those that influenced them. Similarweb data cited in IAB’s report found consumers who received a brand recommendation from an AI assistant are 2.5 times more likely to visit that brand’s website within seven days, but only 8.8% of those visits come directly from AI referrals. Instead, 55.9% are attributed to search and 19.9% to direct traffic.

The IAB framework focuses on three areas. It classifies who or what created the intent, made the decision, and executed the transaction. It defines additional claims AI involvement creates, and the evidence each one requires. And it highlights the transparency and measurement gaps that make those claims difficult to verify. (More detail is available in the framework).

The clearest case of this gap is when publisher content is behind an AI recommendation, the IAB noted in its report. If a consumer asks an AI answer engine for the best option in a category, and the answer draws on a publisher’s review, the consumer may not click the citation (a link to the publisher’s review), and it gets reported as a search or direct sale by the retailer.

“The review shaped the sale, and the publisher is eligible for a contributory claim. The evidence differs depending on how publisher content informed the answer. Content used in model training is generally not traceable from outside the model. Grounding and citation are more observable: publishers may be able to see when their content is cited or reflected in an AI response, although citation alone does not establish how much that content influenced the answer. What remains largely missing is the connection between those sourcing signals and downstream conversions. Until that link exists, the contributory claim cannot be fully substantiated,” the IAB report reads.

The IAB’s recommendations span AI platforms, brands, agencies and publishers. Platforms should disclose whether recommendations are paid or organic, which sources inform them and label AI-originated traffic consistently.

Brands and agencies should apply the framework’s claim-and-evidence standards across platforms, while publishers should identify claims about their content’s influence and the evidence needed to support them, per the report.

“Publishers are feeding the answers but they don’t have the receipts,” said Jaime Schultheis, head of global data partnerships at Bombora, where she leads a network of B2B publishers, brand sites and data providers.

The IAB framework does accomplish defining the parts of the AI measurement journey, which can help publishers compare these between vendors and their different measurement structures, Jon Roberts, People Inc.’s chief innovation officer, said during the roundtable.

“Everyone’s pitching you on their custom proprietary measurement solution, and they’re all using the same terms, and the terms all mean something different because they’ve all designed these in a silo and are racing to get your business, and they’re all unique,” he said.

There are some signs that the measurement gap may narrow soon. The U.K.’s Competition and Markets Authority (CMA) is requiring Google to provide U.K. publishers with clickthrough data for its AI search features by Dec. 3. OpenAI is also testing an analytics dashboard that counts how often a business shows up in ChatGPT’s answers and how many clicks those appearances send to its site.

“We’re waiting [with] bated breath,” said Graham Howes, svp of distribution and AI intelligence at BDG, adding that this data from OpenAI and Google would be incredibly valuable for its GEO offering.

During the roundtable, Roberts noted that without this data, publishers are effectively “flying blind.” Sharing analytics benefits both publishers and AI platforms by helping them identify business opportunities, improve products and better serve their users, he said. Third-party measurement would still be important for independently verifying platforms’ own data, Roberts added.

Brands want this measurement gap solved just as much as publishers. Ross Bucholc, associate media director at Prudential, said in the roundtable that the lack of first-party data from AI platforms is holding back his ability to unlock more ad budgets going to LLMs.

Even with better visibility into AI activity, connecting that activity to sales remains difficult. Josh Blyska, head of insights at Profound, told Digiday that existing attribution tools struggle to connect AI search’s influence on consumer purchase decisions to conversions. Platforms like Profound can measure AI visibility, citations and how brands appear in AI answers, but can’t reliably track whether those interactions lead to purchases.

In a recent analysis of browser-session data, Profound found one in three consumers had a relevant AI conversation about their purchase within a month that went unattributed, Blyska said. (The dataset didn’t include Google’s AI Mode or AI Overviews.)

“It’s a really tricky problem right now,” he said.

Ultimately, the challenge is not just measuring a brand’s visibility within AI platforms but proving that visibility drives business results. John Sills, head of sales, North America at The Guardian, said the industry needs a more consistent way to understand how AI patterns surface and answer questions.

“We’re not measuring CTRs here. There’s no way of really understanding where the attribution is coming from,” he said. “These LLMs are in the ad business now… In the same way you can call your account manager at Facebook and try to optimize a social campaign or on YouTube, we need that level of account management to help guide the best practices.”

“We are capable, and AI companies are capable of reaching deals that give us fair compensation for content and referring to our site. Ninety-five percent of the AI referrals we get now come through OpenAI. So to me, that’s a win for them and a win for us if that’s how people want to access the news. So when people say deals like that aren’t possible or we don’t deserve compensation, I don’t really buy it.”

— Matt Murray, Washington Post executive editor, speaking about its deal with OpenAI, at Web Summit in Lisbon, Portugal.

$330 million: The reported price under consideration for The New York Times Company’s potential acquisition of Letterboxd, an online community for film enthusiasts.

2.7 billion: The number of times Ziff Davis’ content was cited in Google’s AI Overviews and AI Mode in Q3, nearly 10 times the clicks from Google Search those sites received.

37%: Ziff Davis’ YOY decline in Google referrals.

44%: BuzzFeed’s total YOY revenue decline in Q3 2026.

As publishers await click data, Google’s AI visibility push complicates the opt-out decision

WTF is the AI training opt-out policy token?

ChatGPT is about to show brands their mention count

Creators see promise in Instagram’s long-form push, but question whether exposure is enough

Meta indefinitely paused controversial changes to its terms of service that would have allowed it to scrape publishers’ websites, following widespread backlash from news organizations, legal challenges and regulatory scrutiny, Nieman Lab reported.

Press Forward, a philanthropic initiative supporting local journalism, has invested nearly $600 million in news organizations two years ahead of schedule and plans to expand its funding, the AP reported.

Anthropic struck its first content licensing deal with a news publisher, the Associated Press, to provide real-time election data in Claude during the U.S. midterms, signaling a potential shift in the AI company’s longstanding reluctance to pay publishers for their content, Adweek reported.

Viktor Gyökeres makes Arsenal future clear as striker responds to exit speculation

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Viktor Gyökeres has put to bed any doubts over his future at Arsenal by stating his desire to stay.

The striker has been under question over his long-term future under Mikel Arteta after a difficult start to the 2026/27 season. His latest comments suggest he will not be seeking a move away from north London.

Gyökeres moved to Arsenal for £63.5m from Sporting Lisbon last summer. He’s played a key role in the club winning the Premier League last season with 14 league goals.

However, he has not featured as much this season. Kai Havertz often being Arteta’s go-to man centrally, resulting in him only starting once in the Premier League.

This has led to some speculation that the Sweden international may look at his options in January. However, his latest interview with the Swedish outlet Fotbollskanalen suggests otherwise.

Gyökeres was asked if he wants to stay at Arsenal and has made it clear he wants to continue his career at the Emirates.

The striker also spoke about his relationship with Arteta.

He explained that they regularly discuss football, the team’s approach and the opposition. Those conversations also help him understand what Arteta expects when he is selected to start.

It is a key point to note that just because he hasn’t had much time on the pitch doesn’t mean there have been any issues between the two.

Gyökeres has begun to show his worth again in recent weeks. He found the net against Fleetwood in the League Cup and then Slavia Prague in the Champions League.

This came after a long time to get his first goal of the season and gives him the chance to remind Arteta what he can bring to the team.

There is no doubt that Gyökeres has not lived up to the expectations he had when he came from Portugal.

But his overall effect cannot be ignored. He has contributed 23 goals and 6 assists in 69 games for Arsenal.

The challenge will be finding a way to get the best out of him and Havertz. They add different attributes, and Arteta will have plenty of options to choose from depending on the opposition.

They will also want to avoid losing someone they have invested heavily in so early on. Gyökeres has a contract until 2030, so they are in a great position.

His latest comments will help to reassure fans that he is not looking to leave the club after stating his ambition to play more games and make himself an indispensable part of Arteta’s squad.

The main thing for Arsenal will be to make sure that he can turn this attitude into consistent performances. Gyökeres has made his intentions clear and now needs to show why they brought him to North London in the first place.

Archive, 1990: Peter Shilton wins 125th cap making him England’s record appearance holder

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

9 July 1990: The goalkeeper’s 20-year international career ends at the Italia 90 World Cup

Harry Kane is expected to break the record for England men’s appearances tonight against Croatia at Wembley in the Nations League, overtaking goalkeeper Peter Shilton’s 125 appearances, a record set nearly four decades ago at the World Cup in Italy.

Shilton wore the captain’s armband in his final England appearance in the unloved third-place play-off against Italy in Bari. The Three Lions had previously lost on penalties to West Germany in the semi-finals.

The Guardian’s Ian Ridley, pitch side, reported that Shilton, who announced his retirement from international football that night, said: “I would just like to say how much I have thoroughly enjoyed the last 20 years.”

That joy must have been tempered slightly, though, when, in the 71st minute, Shilton, who had made his international debut against East Germany in 1970, untypically lost concentration, allowing Roberto Baggio to steal the ball as he casually cleared a back pass and scored Italy’s opening goal.

Despite a pinpoint headed equaliser by David Platt, England went down 2-1, with Schillaci scoring a penalty to win the tournament’s Golden Boot. It was also England manager Bobby Robson’s last game in charge. On his rare error, Shilton said of his teammates: “No one, though, seemed to mind much.”

It was goodnight from him and it was goodnight from him. Bobby Robson ended his eight years as keeper of the England keys and Peter Shilton his career as keeper of the England goal, after 125 appearances, in footballing debit but moral credit.

At times, too, it must have seemed like a custodial sentence for Robson, but not on a windy, moonlit Saturday night by the Adriatic at the San Nicola stadium. The loss to Italy was honourable, like the man and certainly in this tournament the team.

The draping banners for Italy saying “Thanks anyway” applied equally to England. “Bravissimo” was the verdict on them of Azeglio Vicini, the host nation’s manager. Robson exuded bonhomie, except that he could not resist a bit of manager-speak about the two goals “which I’m not very happy about”.

Unfortunately for Shilton, 30 minutes before announcing his retirement “I would just like to say how much I have thoroughly enjoyed the last 20 years” his error gave Italy their first goal. No one, though, seemed to mind much.

Robson “made” Shilton play because he wanted him to finish at the top, even though the venerable one was ready to stand aside for Woods. For Robson “I shall miss it greatly. It has been my life for eight years” it was “nice” that the two should end at the same time. ‘No doubt we’ll both be missed,” he added with a smile.

Shilton saved excellently from Ferrara in the first half, tipping his shot on to a post, but after 70 minutes found himself Gary Crosbyed by Baggio as he tried to dribble the ball. Baggio was offside when Schillaci fed him the ball to cut inside Parker and score, but then again Shilton had brought him down as punishment for robbing him. It all evened out.

So did the game 10 minutes later, England’s favourite time. The next manager might even consider beginning his team talk: ‘Right lads. Just imagine we’re one down and there’s 10 minutes to go ...’

Dorigo, who had an excellent first start proving again that as a left-back he is an outstanding left-winger crossed and Platt beat Baresi and his ball-watching defence to thump home a header.

No one could contemplate extra-time, least of all the referee Joel Quiniou, it seemed. Having apparently decided earlier that these teams had had enough of penalties hence his decision to ignore Ancelotti’s handling of Beardsley’s free-kick he changed his mind with five minutes left.

Schillaci fell over Walker’s legs and the ‘little hero’, as Robson called him, stepped up to move out on his own as the top scorer of the tournament with his sixth goal. Italy were denied a third in injury-time when a badly erring linesman flagged Berti offside after he had beautifully headed home Baggio’s cross.

England played their part in an entertaining game. It was less tame than might have been expected and was certainly taken seriously by both sides.

The record will show that Robson’s teams played 95, won 47, drew 29 and lost 19 and that Shilton was one of three Bryan Robson and Butcher the others who spanned his tenure. Indeed, it was the passing of an era, but one that should not be allowed to end.

ITALY: Zenga; Baresi, Bergomi, De Agostini (Berti, 65min), Ferrara, Maldini, Vierchowod, Ancelotti, Giannini (Ferri, 89), Baggio, Schillaci.

ENGLAND: Shilton; Stevens, Walker, Wright (Waddle, 73), Parker, Dorigo, Steven, McMahon (Webb, 73), Platt, Lineker, Beardsley.

Even remote wildlife can’t escape microplastics in their blood

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Scientists found microplastics in the blood of wild tapirs and giant anteaters in Brazil, including animals living far from human development.

As the afternoon sun began to fade over Brazil’s countryside, a team of researchers drove along dusty dirt roads searching for giant anteaters.

Two spotters rode in the back of the truck, scanning the landscape for signs of movement. When they spotted an anteater, the team approached on foot with two long-handled nets.

Capturing the animal usually took about a minute, but the information gathered would help answer a much bigger question: How far has plastic pollution spread into the natural world?

The researchers were investigating whether wild animals living closer to human activity had more microplastics in their blood.

Their findings revealed a clear connection between human disturbance and plastic exposure.

But one discovery was particularly concerning.

Even in Brazil’s remote Pantanal, the world’s largest tropical wetland, every animal sampled had microplastics in its blood, despite living more than 40 miles (65 kilometers) from the nearest development.

The results suggest that plastic pollution is reaching wildlife even in places that appear largely untouched by humans.

Scientists had widely assumed a connection between human activity and plastic in wildlife.

To test it, the researchers needed several records for the same animals, namely blood samples, GPS movements and detailed maps of human disturbance.

Study co-author Justin Calabrese of the Center for Advanced Systems Understanding (CASUS) described the difficulty of getting all those records together.

“Such integrated datasets simply did not exist,” Calabrese said.

Marcia de Almeida Monteiro Melo Ferraz and Michael Noonan, both at the University of British Columbia Okanagan, conceived the study.

Teams from two long-running conservation programs in Brazil did the fieldwork.

From February 2021 to November 2022, those teams collected blood from 26 lowland tapirs and 21 giant anteaters.

The animals lived at a soy farm on the Amazon’s deforestation frontier, farmland in the Cerrado savanna and a cattle ranch in the Pantanal.

For the anteaters, the work took place near two federal highways, where the Anteaters and Highways Project studies collisions between vehicles and wildlife.

Each blood sample went into a glass tube, which the field team sealed with aluminum foil to prevent contamination.

To record where the animals went, adult tapirs wore GPS collars that logged an hourly position. The anteaters wore GPS trackers as well.

Once in the lab, each sample went through a chemical treatment to break down the blood. The team collected the solids left behind on a filter.

Raman spectroscopy then identified the materials from the way they scattered light from a laser.

When the team had checked all 47 blood samples, 43 contained microplastics. The particles averaged about 0.0004 inches (10 micrometers) in length.

Pieces of that size need a microscope to be seen.

Counts for individual animals ran from zero to more than 1,200 particles in a milliliter of blood. The average for all the animals was about 74, and a milliliter is roughly a fifth of a teaspoon.

For comparison, the average fell within the range already reported in human blood by other studies using spectroscopy.

The tapirs had about 99 particles per milliliter on average and the anteaters about 43. The difference between the species was not statistically significant.

GPS records provided more than 440,000 locations, allowing researchers to map the home ranges of 40 animals.

To describe the land within those areas the researchers used the Human Footprint Index.

This global map has scores running from zero for undisturbed land up to 50 for the most heavily modified.

An animal did not need to live across uniformly disturbed land for a link to appear.

The team found more particles in blood when the most heavily modified spot in an animal’s range had a higher footprint score.

Average footprint scores across entire home ranges were not significantly associated with microplastic levels.

The distance comparisons also pointed to less plastic in animals living farther from development, which here meant urban areas, mines and power plants.

The same was true of animals living farther from waste management facilities.

Calabrese said the paper “is the first to demonstrate this linkage for terrestrial mammals,” meaning mammals that live on land.

The researchers also checked how much the highest values influenced the findings. They ran each test again without the animals making up the top 10% for that measure.

For the distance tests, that meant leaving out the animals living farthest away.

After that check the footprint link remained, while neither distance link was still statistically significant.

Animals with more farmland in their home ranges did not have higher microplastic levels in their blood.

The authors suggested that farmland may add less direct exposure to microplastics than other kinds of human disturbance.

The types of plastic offered another comparison between sites, with differences the authors described as consistent with local land use.

Animals at the Amazon farm carried more polyamide, the main material in fishing nets and ropes, than animals on Cerrado farmland.

The Cerrado animals had more PVC, a material used in irrigation pipes, than animals in the Pantanal.

The authors described the site comparisons as case studies, with uneven numbers of animals at each site.

For the animals at the cattle ranch in the Pantanal, the distance from the nearest waste facility was about 70 miles (112 kilometers). Each of them still carried microplastics in its blood.

The researchers also obtained a value of about 10 particles per milliliter of blood from their model for land with a footprint score of zero.

The authors suggested that the value may partly reflect limitations of the model.

Even so, they wrote that direct contact with land changed by people was unlikely to be the only route into wildlife.

The authors identified wind and water as likely routes carrying microplastics into remote habitats. Cities, industry and farms supply airborne microplastics that may settle far away.

Runoff, rivers and seasonal floods provide ways for particles to move far from their sources as well.

Microplastics in the water and sediment of the Pantanal were already known from previous studies.

The highest measurements in those studies came from places near rivers flowing out of urban areas.

This observational study found associations between human disturbance and microplastic levels in blood, but it cannot establish cause and effect.

The researchers had a single blood sample from each animal. Their method could not identify particles smaller than about 0.00004 inches (one micrometer).

Future studies could collect blood, soil and water samples from the same locations to help trace the particles’ sources.

The team did not collect those environmental samples and could not yet match the plastic in blood to its source.

The measurements also concern the amount of plastic, without examining its effects on the health of these wild animals. Laboratory experiments have shown that microplastics can harm animals.

For the years 2001 to 2020, the increase in Brazil’s average footprint score was 42%, whereas the worldwide increase was 26%.

Thus, as land development continues, the authors suggested that the plastic levels in land animals would continue to increase.

They described keeping wildlife farther from development and waste facilities as a possible way to lower the amount the animals carry.

The study is published in the journal Nature.

Nine Korean startups vie for a spot in Disrupt 2027’s Startup Battlefield 200 competition

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

TechCrunch’s yearly Startup Battlefield 200 competition sees hundreds of companies from across the world trying every year to get a chance to pitch in front of VCs and the international tech community at the Disrupt conference.

This week, nine early-stage South Korean startups pitched at DETO 2026, a technology conference in Seoul, to earn a spot in next year’s Startup Battlefield 200.

The startups work across the deep tech sector, including healthcare, fintech, energy, semiconductors, and AI. All nine startups are looking to enter the U.S. market as part of the broader international expansion plans.

Voinosis, which develops AI to detect changes in brain health through voice analysis, won the competition. The other eight startups are listed below in alphabetical order.

Voinosis is developing an AI tool that analyzes speech patterns such as pitch, pauses, rhythm and response times to detect changes linked to brain health. Each voice check takes less than five minutes.

The company says its technology has been tested in Greek, Japanese and English, and its product doesn’t require separate training for each language. In a clinical study involving 146 participants, the startup claims its tech achieved 88% accuracy in screening for potential cognitive issues.

Voinosis targets hospitals, senior living facilities, insurers and individual consumers. It also plans to link voice checks with cognitive training, assessments, and treatment provided by healthcare professionals.

The startup was founded by Grace Shin, who spent more than 25 years as an otologic surgeon. The startup is currently raising a Series A round and has already secured a South Korean pharmaceutical company as a strategic investor.

Awesome Lab, based in Seoul, makes ThermoFlux, a heating system that uses water electrolysis and ionization to generate heat. The system can manage heat production for buildings, water supply, and industrial processes. The company says its technology can reduce energy consumption and carbon emissions.

For its international expansion, Awesome Lab is taking a different approach in each market. In the U.S., it plans to supply core OEM modules, and work with boiler, water heater and HVAC companies on product development.

Crosshub helps travelers and international visitors verify their identities and set up payment methods abroad. Its platform, IDBlock, combines identity verification and payment services using Self-Sovereign Identity (SSI) and zero-knowledge proof.

The company says its platform helps users prove their identity across borders without sharing unnecessary personal information. Crosshub is also integrating this technology with payment systems to improve access to financial services for travelers.

DeepFusion AI has developed a 4D radar-based imaging system with additional sensors for autonomous mobility, robotaxi, maritime surveillance, and defense applications. The system combines RF sensors with radar and lidar to detect objects, movement, or distance without a direct line of sight. All sensors are supposedly packed into a single, customizable unit.

EFLOW develops special motors that can produce more torque, or rotational force, while taking up less space for use in robots, drones, satellites, and other mobility applications. The startup also develops actuators that control how robotic joints move.

Otition Medical makes two medical devices: one specializes in measuring body temperature, while another can capture ear canal images and analyze the eardrum. The company says its AI was trained on about 55,000 images of eardrums, and the device has received medical certification in South Korea.

Riibotics says it can deploy robots into warehouses within a day. Riibotics wants to reduce deployment costs for warehouses by streamlining setup, which includes infrastructure setup, warehouse mapping, system integration through custom middleware, task programming, and safety checks.

Ternell, a semiconductor startup based in Ulsan, is developing a new type of memory to help AI chips use less power.

Unlike conventional two-state memory, Ternell’s T-SRAM system adds a third state for more efficient data storage. Founded in 2019 by KyungRok Kim, a former Samsung Electronics engineer, Ternell reports that tests with 28-nanometer chips showed a 33% to 102% increase in memory capacity per area, and a 55% to 71% reduction in energy needed to access data, compared with conventional SRAM.

Testbank is a Korean edtech startup developing Solve, an app that converts printed study materials, such as workbooks and test-prep books, into interactive digital versions.

The startup uses AI to identify question and answer spaces in publishers’ PDF files while preserving the original layout. Solve lets you write answers directly on the page and be graded automatically.

DTCC to Bring Tokenized Stocks and Treasuries to Solana in 2027

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

DTCC to Bring Tokenized Stocks and Treasuries to Solana in 2027

The Depository Trust & Clearing Corporation (DTCC), the backbone of U.S. securities settlement, plans to bring its tokenized securities to Solana, it announced on Thursday.

The firm will connect the DTCC Tokenization Service (DTS) to the Solana network, allowing eligible assets held by its Depository Trust Company (DTC) subsidiary to be converted into tokens on Solana. The integration is planned for the second half of 2027.

BREAKING: @The_DTCC to tokenize custodied assets on Solana. announced plans to connect the DTCC Tokenization Service to Solana, enabling the tokenization of eligible DTC-custodied assets on the network.

The integration is planned for the second half of 2027, further advancing… pic.twitter.com/gXszWFgUOX

"DTCC is bringing regulated, DTC-tokenized securities to another major blockchain ecosystem, giving market participants greater choice in where they access and utilize tokenized assets," DTCC CEO Frank La Salla said.

DTS went live on October 28 on the Canton Network and DTCC's own private blockchain, built on the Besu framework, with more networks due to be added in 2027, according to DTCC's launch announcement. The service creates tokenized "digital twins" of securities held at DTC, which custodies more than $114 trillion in assets. Solana joins Stellar and Circle's Arc blockchain, which are expected to be added in 2027, according to a DTCC fact sheet.

Under a no-action letter from the SEC, the service is limited to a defined set of highly liquid assets, including Russell 1000 stocks, ETFs tracking major indexes, and U.S. Treasuries. Morgan Stanley said last week it had tokenized U.S. stocks and Treasuries through the service.

Tokens issued on Solana "will retain the same investor protections, ownership rights and regulatory safeguards that apply to traditionally held securities," said Brian Steele, president of DTCC Clearing & Securities Services.

Solana has been actively courting traditional finance in recent months, with Solana Foundation head of institutional Nick Ducoff arguing that, "Institutions choose Solana because it already runs real financial activity at scale." He added that institutions are "already issuing, settling and deploying capital" on the network, in a tweet shared by Solana's official account.

Last month, the Solana Foundation unveiled an open standard for institutional settlement developed with input from J.P. Morgan, and the network completed its Alpenglow upgrade, cutting the time it takes for transactions to become final to roughly 150 milliseconds.

Solana is also lining up more venues for tokenized stocks. Trading platform Formation has notified the SEC of plans to trade tokenized U.S. stocks on Solana, starting with Galaxy and Forward Industries.
