Alphabet reportedly raises $3.8B in first Hong Kong and Singapore bonds

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Orders topped $7B, letting Google’s parent upsize from a $3B target as it hunts new currencies to fund its AI buildout.

Alphabet is raising about $3.8B through its first-ever Hong Kong and Singapore dollar bonds, according to term sheets seen by Reuters, beating its own target after investors placed more than $7B in orders.

Google’s parent is selling HK$16B ($2B) of Hong Kong dollar bonds and S$2.28B ($1.8B) in Singapore dollars across seven tranches. It had aimed for $3B combined, per Reuters’ reporting on Wednesday, but order books exceeding HK$32.5B and S$4B let it upsize, with pricing tightening across every maturity.

The Hong Kong paper runs from two-year bonds at a 4.35% reoffer yield to 10-year bonds at 5.20%; the Singapore side stretches to a 30-year tranche at 3.80%. All came in tighter than initial guidance. The bonds settle on November 19 and are expected to be rated Aa2 by Moody’s and AA+ by S&P.

HSBC and JPMorgan coordinated the sale, with Bank of China (Hong Kong), ICBC, and Standard Chartered arranging the Hong Kong leg, and DBS, OCBC, and United Overseas Bank the Singapore one. Proceeds go to general corporate purposes, which may include repaying debt.

That last clause is almost funny, because the debt keeps coming. Alphabet raised €6B in euro bonds last week, sold $25B in August in a US deal it said would become a twice-yearly habit, and added A$5.5B in Australian dollars the same month.

Over the past two years it has borrowed in dollars, euros, pounds, Swiss francs, yen, and Canadian dollars, and even sold a 100-year bond, taking its total issuance since the start of 2025 past $114B, the most of any AI-related borrower.

The cause sits on the balance sheet. Alphabet has lifted its 2026 capital budget to as much as $205B for data centres and chips, and last year posted its first negative free cash flow since its 2004 listing.

Cash alone no longer covers the AI bill, for Alphabet or anyone else: Big Tech’s AI debt pile passed $350B this summer, and the IMF says hyperscalers now account for a tenth of the world’s investment-grade bond issuance, up from under 1% in 2024.

Hence the novelty currencies. Each new market is a fresh pool of lenders that has not yet had its fill of AI paper, and Hong Kong and Singapore savers are merely the latest. The order books say the appetite is still there; the pace of issuance says Alphabet intends to test exactly how deep it runs.

At $10,250, Could This 1995 Mercedes-Benz E320 Estate Bring Class To Car Line?

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

It's arguable that the W124-series was the last of the bank-vault-era Benzes. Today's Nice Price or No Dice E320 estate is one of the most desirable of those vaults to potentially invest in, and it has the rare third row. Let's see if its price tag has us opening up our hypothetical bank accounts.

Perhaps the polar opposite of a Mercedes of any era in build quality, safety, and engineering is the backyard-built kit car. Arising in the late 1950s and carrying through its heyday in the 1970s, home-built kit cars typically started with a donor chassis, often from an old Triumph or VW. A fresh fiberglass body gave that chassis new life, and the builder a sense of pride in creating a working, drivable car.

The Meyers Manx SR2 we looked at on Wednesday was an example—and an extremely rare one at that—of a '70s-era kit car. Created by industry legend Bruce Meyers, the SR2 proved to be his swan song, as his company folded shortly after its launch. That makes the small clutch of original SR2s (about 200 total) far more dear when they do come on the market. Our SR2 came with Corvair power and an interior ready for the next owner's taste in upholstery. At $17,000, however, that wasn't a winning combo. You all expected more for that amount and sent the Manx packing with a massive 93% 'No Dice' loss.

The closest today's 1995 Mercedes-Benz E320 Estate comes to a kit car is that it's the same model year Mercedes-Benz India, Ltd. began building similar models from knock-down kits at its Chakan assembly plant. This is also nearly the last year for the W124 series as a whole, as it was replaced, body style by body style, over the course of the next three years: 1995 (saloons), 1996 (estates), 1997 (coupes), and 1998 (cabriolets). Mercedes is nothing if not methodical.

This generation, which represents the facelifted version of the W124, was also the first to carry Mercedes' new naming convention of a class letter followed by a number indicating—sometimes only semi-accurately—engine displacement. The company has carried on this convention to this day. Because of that simple nomenclature, we can tell from the name that this is a mid-size E-Class (Executive class) model with a 3.2-liter engine. See? Easy-peasy.

That 3.2-liter engine is an M104.992 straight-six with double-overhead cams and four valves per cylinder. In North American guise, it produces a workmanlike 217 horsepower and 229 pound-feet of torque. As in all U.S.-bound cars, here it's matched with a five-speed 4G-Tronic automatic actuated through Mercedes' traditional pixelated snake shift gate.

According to the ad, this car has undergone significant repairs and maintenance over its 159,850 miles. The seller notes that under their ownership, they replaced the engine wiring harness, head and head gasket, radiator, and water pump. In addition, the car has a new climate control panel and has had two failed window regulators replaced. Other plusses include Continental tires with plenty of tread left and "flawlessly working" A/C.

With all that in the rearview, the seller now describes the car as "sorted and in excellent working condition." The receipts for the work will accompany the car in the sale for the next owner's reading enjoyment.

The car's appearance matches its mechanical status. The exterior is painted Smoke Silver, which the seller notes as paint code 702 for those keeping score. Yes, there will be a test later. The ad claims this Benz isn't a show car, but that it's solid enough to take out in public without embarrassment. On the downside, there are noticeable scratches and wear-through areas on the rear bumper and rocker moldings, but otherwise the paint looks to be in top-notch shape. Factory eight-hole alloys lend the car an all-original look, but could stand some work with a Scotchbrite to bring out their best nature.

In the cabin, the seller notes in the ad that the driver's seat shows wear and, wisely (sneakily?), doesn't show us how extensive it is. The rest of the cabin presents well, and the desirable rear-facing foldable third row is present and accounted for. If we're picking nits, we can point out that the (real) wood on the center console around the shifter is showing signs of losing battles with the sun. The seller, clearly appreciative of the car overall, claims it "wears its patina with pride."

On the plus side, a more modern stereo has been installed, but it's a Mercedes unit, so it keeps the vibe alive. Other plusses to consider: the car has a clean title, a passing smog test grade, and carries current registration, so there's no back-due malarkey for the next owner.

Apparently, under its current owner, this Benz has not been pressed into daily driver duty, as the seller claims to use it "as a Sunday driver and for the occasional school run." That's an acceptable compromise to keep the wear and tear at a minimum while still letting the car get some exercise. Based on the seller's description, though, this seems like a solid car to daily, and what a classy ride it would be.

Naturally, that class comes at a cost: $10,250. It should be noted that Mercedes' E-Class estates and their earlier W123 siblings are highly desirable in the present market, making them pricier than the average bear.

The question for you is whether or not this seemingly well-sorted estate is worth the seller's $10,250 asking. What do you think? Is that a fair deal for a classy classic? Or is that too much for something taking pride in its patina?

Los Angeles, California, Craigslist, or go here if the ad disappears.

Oil prices mixed after Trump says no Iran deal needed: 'We've got their Strait'

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

U.S. oil prices traded around the flatline Thursday, as comments from President Donald Trump stoked worries that an end to the U.S.-Iran war was not in sight.

Brent crude traded 0.5% higher at $93.29 per barrel at 9:09 a.m. West Texas Intermediate futures gained slipped 0.1% to trade at $86.05 per barrel.

"We don't need a deal with Iran. Those days have long since passed. We've got their money, Hundreds of Billions of Dollars, and we've got their Strait!" Trump said in a Truth Social post.

Thursday's gains follow a sharp pullback after Trump played down the prospect of further strikes on Iran after the midterm elections. WTI is down about 16% in November, according to data from FactSet.

On Sunday Trump said that fresh attacks were highly unlikely as talks to end the conflict progressed, despite both ⁠countries reporting that shipping had been hit in or near the Strait of Hormuz. On Monday, he said further military action did not appear necessary "at this point," easing concerns about prolonged disruptions to Middle East oil supplies.

John Kilduff, a partner at Again Capital, said investors may have been too quick to price in an end to the fighting.

"The market may have gotten ahead of itself on pricing in a cessation of hostilities," Kilduff told CNBC. He warned that renewed doubts about a deal would push the security premium in oil prices higher.

Trump's assertion that the U.S. controls the Strait of Hormuz could spur further Iranian attacks on shipping, increasing the risk of prolonged supply disruptions, according to Kilduff.

"The President may be thinking that given enough time, the economic blockade of Iran will force them to either negotiate with the USA or result in a regime change as the Iranian economy collapses. The Iranians might have different ideas and continue to attack Middle East Infrastructure that results in higher prices and forces the President to the negotiating table," said Andy Lipow, president of Lipow Oil Associates.

"In any event, it does not seem to me that either side's negotiating position has changed. Iran wants the blockade to end, sanctions to be lifted and a ceasefire including Lebanon to be declared. The USA wants Iran to get rid of its entire nuclear stockpile and allow free transit through the Strait of Hormuz," he said.

—CNBC's Pippa Stevens contributed to the report.

GTA 6 South Korean Age Rating Confirms Previous Leaks Were A Dev Build

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

South Korea almost refused to give Grand Theft Auto 6 an age rating due to the overwhelming amount of nudity “without context” that looked even worse thanks to Cyberleek.

I can’t believe we’re finally here, but we’re now officially one week away from the release of GTA 6. It’s been a long, long road, but things have finally started to feel real over the past few months as the game’s final trailers, marketing, collaborations, screenshot batches, and more have emerged to show just how far Rockstar’s marketing can go.

On the more official end, age-rating boards have started to share their ratings for GTA 6, unsurprisingly confirming that it’s getting the highest possible classifications across the board. That just about includes South Korea, which apparently almost outright refused to give the game a rating due in part to its “background genital exposure”.

As pointed out by ResetEra user TheAbsolution, Inven Global has shared a report on just how close GTA 6 got to being refused classification in South Korea. You might think that it’s because of the game’s more realistic violence or drug use, but that’s not the case, as the board deemed that it wasn’t too much more advanced than GTA 5.

Exactly how realistic is Rockstar's long-awaited Grand Theft Auto 6 going to be?

The problem lies with the game’s more frequent nudity, specifically without any “sexual context”, essentially referring to background characters who are naked in-game. One member of the board pointed out that the nudity was an issue because it “has never appeared this frequently without context” or with “narrative justification”.

The report also shows that there was some pressure to figure it out so that South Korea wasn’t the only country to refuse to give GTA 6 a rating.

South Korea’s rating board’s first meeting ended with a tie and resulted in a copy being given for review to evaluate “sensuality”. The board found that the “genital exposure scenes” appeared as one-offs in the background that didn’t lead to any interactions. The board even had to request a map from Take-Two multiple times to find the nudist gathering that was previously leaked.

Eventually, the decision was made to give GTA 6 an Adults Only rating, which was in part due to the infamous leaked nudity scenes from Cyberleek not being found in-game, confirming once and for all that it was a dev build hack and not meant to be seen. It’s not clear if any modifications ended up being made by Rockstar or Take-Two, but at least a classification has been issued.

How Pep Guardiola feels about USA job after Mauricio Pochettino to Man Utd manager update

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

By Oliver Thomas | 12 Nov 2026 13:22

The USA national team would like to appoint Pep Guardiola as their new manager to succeed Manchester United-bound Mauricio Pochettino, according to a report.

The Red Devils officially confirmed the sacking of head coach Michael Carrick on Thursday and it is understood that an agreement in principle has been reached for Pochettino to take the reins at Old Trafford.

Man United are said to have made contact with the USA and are willing to pay the £7.4m release clause in Pochettino’s contract, though the Red Devils would be comfortable with him taking charge of international matches against Haiti on Saturday and Wednesday.

USA would have ideally liked to have retained the services of Pochettino, who was appointed head coach in September 2024 and oversaw the country’s 2026 World Cup campaign before signing a contract extension in August to keep him in charge until the summer of 2030.

However, The Athletic reports that former Barcelona, Bayern Munich and Manchester City boss Guardiola is expected to become a ‘top target’ for the USA national team in the event that Pochettino moves to Man United.

Their pursuit of Guardiola would not be straightforward, though, as the Catalan has shown little interest in an imminent return to management since ending his legendary 10-year career at the Etihad Stadium in the summer.

Guardiola turned down the opportunity to become Italy head coach earlier this year, with the president of the Italian Football Federation (FIGC), Giovanni Malago, telling La Repubblica: “Pep was honest. He took a sabbatical, partly for family reasons, but if he’d done anything, he would have done it with us.”

Nevertheless, Guardiola is said to have previously had dialogue with USA, and has long been intrigued by the possibility of working in America.

Guardiola was allegedly contacted by USA, along with Jurgen Klopp, before they ended up hiring Pochettino to replace Gregg Berhalter in 2024.

Speaking about USA after Pochettino was appointed, Guardiola told reporters: "The USA is fascinating to try to build a project for many years and be able to get the national team to the next step.

“The potential with the USA is there, given the number of people and sports, and so on.

"I wish [Pochettino] and the USA - I have friends there - the best of luck. I know he's a top-class manager, an experienced manager."

Following his 2012 departure from Barcelona, Guardiola spent a year-long sabbatical in New York City, living with his family in a luxury apartment near Central Park where he enjoyed a rare sense of anonymity.

After a decade in charge of Man City – where he won 20 trophies including six Premier League titles – Guardiola has since become a Global Ambassador for the club and stated how he plans to take an extended break from management.

"After my contract with City, I'm going to stop. I'm sure," he said in May 2025. "I don't know if I'm going to retire, but I'm going to take a break. How I want to be remembered, I don't know."

In a separate interview, he added: "So I'm not retiring, but I will take a break, for sure. When I finish here, I will take a break. I need to make a stop, that's all.”

As well as the USA, Guardiola has been tipped to manage England at some stage in the future, though Thomas Tuchel is currently in charge of the Three Lions and is expected to stay at least until the end of the 2028 European Championship.

Paradox boss Frederik Wester stepping down after several tumultuous years for the Cities Skylines and Stellaris publisher

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Deputy CEO Mattias Lilja taking over role.

Paradox Interactive's Frederik Wester has announced he's stepping down from the role of CEO after a combined decade and a half leading the company. The news follows several tumultuous years for the publisher best known for grand strategy games including Stellaris, Europa Universalis, and Crusader Kings 3.

In a statement announcing the change in leadership, Paradox said Wester had "decided to step down as CEO to continue focusing on the company's long-term development as a member of the Board." Paradox's current deputy CEO, Mattias Lilja, will take over Wester's role from 4th January 2027. Wester is to remain in the position until then in order "to ensure an orderly transition".

"I'm truly grateful to all our engaged players and talented colleagues who contribute to outstanding gaming experiences every day," Wester said of his decision. "They are the ones who made these years amazing. My plan was never to be CEO for life. The goal was to build the foundation before passing the baton on for the next chapter in the company's journey. Since I returned as CEO, we've streamlined our strategy, rebuilt our pipeline and developed our commercial offering. Mattias has had a central role in laying this foundation, and now is the right time to hand over. Given his experience and vision, he is exceptionally well positioned to take the company to its next level."

Wester has twice served as Paradox CEO since joining the company in 2003. His first stint ran from 2009 to 2018, after which he became Paradox's executive chairman of the board. He returned to the CEO role in 2021, and has overseen a troubled period for the company. This has included the embattled development of Vampire: The Masquerade Bloodlines 2 - which finally launched last October after multiple delays, near cancellation, and a switch in developer. Unfortunately the game failed to resonate with critics and audiences, and Paradox announced a 162 percent decline in operating profit earlier this year as a result of its commercial failure.

Wester's most recent watch - which began with an admittance of "inappropriate behaviour" amid a damning report into Paradox's workplace culture - has also seen the high-profile cancellation of Sims competitor Life By You, an indefinite delay for Prison Architect 2 after Paradox parted ways with original developer Double Eleven, and a split with BattleTech studio Harebrained Schemes following the commercial failure of The Lamplighters League. Paradox's Cities Skylines 2 has also been blighted with controversy: after a disastrous launch in 2023, its console release is still yet to emerge, and development is now being handled by a new studio after Paradox split with former developer Colossal Order last year. In 2004, Wester admitted the publisher had "made the wrong calls in several projects".

Despite all this, incoming CEO Mattias Lilja remains buoyant. "Paradox has a unique DNA that has made us one of the greats in deep and engaging strategy and management games," he wrote in a statement announcing his promotion. "Now we’re stepping up the pace: we will make further investments in our core business, push the boundaries of our game design, work even more closely with our loyal players, develop more and better tools and services that benefit both modders and players, and continue to sharpen our ways of working and our organisation, with player value in focus."

Paradox, does of course, have some promising games on its upcoming slate. Alongside continued work on grand strategy stalwarts like Crusader Kings 3, Europa Universalis 5, and Stellaris, next year sees the release of Lego Skylines, and post-apocalyptic grand strategy game Afterworld is also on the way.

Capello names two Italy players he’s excited to watch and two who need to step up

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Fabio Capello believes that Pio Esposito is already a leader by example in the Italy national team squad, says that he is excited to watch Milan’s Alphadjo Cisse when he is given the chance, and has urged midfielder Nicolo Barella and Sandro Tonali to step us as two of the experienced members of Roberto Mancini’s squad.

The Italy national team will be in Nations League action against France in San Siro at 19.45 GMT on Thursday evening. The two nations played out a 1-1 draw when they met in Paris last month.

As things stand, France lead the Nations League group on 10 points after four matches, Italy are in second on seven points, Belgium in third on six points and Turkey in last on zero.

Capello gave his pre-match assessment of the Nazionale in a column for La Gazzetta dello Sport.

Italy are only four games into Mancini’s second spell as national team head coach, but Capello feels that there have already been encouraging signs.

“While waiting for the Italy-France game tonight, I’m still thinking about the great game the Azzurri played in France, with great personality,” Capello wrote in his Gazzetta dello Sport column.

“From there, I’ve enjoyed our national team a lot and I expect to see a team with the same personality, playing aggressive football in San Siro.

“More than anything, I noticed that there was pride for the shirt, determination to win every ball. There was a willingness to suffer when required and a willingness to help every teammate. Finally, it looked like a glimpse of that team spirit we had been missing.”

It’s fair to say that Italy and France have been on different trajectories since their 2006 World Cup final in Berlin.

“We’re talking about Italy vs. France, which is always special. As we’ve lost our way in recent years, they’ve had some excellent results. 20 years have gone since the 2006 World Cup final and they have lost very few games since then,” Capello wrote.

“For the Azzurri, it will be essential for them to have the ‘can do’ attitude this evening, for them to give everything they can to help each other. That’s one of the most important things, whatever sport we’re talking about.”

Mancini has cut six players from the squad that he named for the November international break in order to comply with the 23-man squad limit for Nations League matches.

Cisse, Mattia Liberali, Federico Gatti, Honest Ahanor, Matteo Palma and Niccolo Pisilli have not made the cut. Still, there could still be opportunities for several youngsters including the likes of Esposito, Marco Palestra, Michael Kayode, Alessandro Romano, Issa Doumbia, Antonio Vergara or Luca Koleosho.

“We expect to see a national team with a few young players, so those more experienced ones will have to show that they’ve been at these levels for longer, and why they deserve that status,” Capello wrote.

“If I think about Pio Esposito, I’m thinking about a player who has already imposed himself, who is already accepted by the whole set-up and has already convinced. He doesn’t yet have the experience to offer the team, but he can set an example for everyone else by fighting, sacrificing, working hard and playing with personality. Either you have that, or life becomes difficult.

“Whether it’s in this game or the next, I’m curious to see Cisse. I agree with what Baldini said: It is a guarantee that he will express himself. I liked Cisse immediately because he offers something different, something more. And if I criticised him for passing backwards recently, it’s because he needs that personality, desire, courage to play, to always go forwards.

“What impresses me the most about him is his ability to hit the ball first time and always stick it in, he’s always well co-ordinated.”

Capello feels that now is the time for Tonali and Barella to step up and prove themselves as leaders.

“I expect a lot from the midfielders, as always from Barella and Tonali downwards. I believe that the two of them have to give something more because they’re the ones who have the most experience and I’m always convinced that it is the midfield that makes the team tick.

“We will have to go well, because we’re up against a very strong team with very few weaknesses. They have quality, speed and aggressiveness, they have it all.

“Then there’s the coach, Zinedine Zidane, who gave us great memories as a footballer here in Italy with Juventus and wants to show that he knows his stuff as a coach as well, as he’s already shown with Real Madrid.

“It was already noticeable in the first few games, when he got frustrated after a poor goal-kick for example, quite how much he cares. It shows that after being an extraordinary footballer, he feels this commitment with the national team even more.”

Peter Young is a Senior Reporter with Football Italia.

Fabrizio Romano Makes Man Utd January Transfer Guarantee as Pochettino Deal Agreed

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Manchester United are set to appoint Mauricio Pochettino as their next manager after sacking Michael Carrick.

The Red Devils have had a poor campaign so far and sit in the relegation zone in the Premier League following their worst ever start to a season, with Carrick having led the team to just one win in their opening ten games.

54-year-old Pochettino has been identified as the man to take over, with Man Utd agreeing terms with him to become their next manager, and talks almost finalised with US Soccer to trigger the $12m (£9m) release clauses to bring him and his staff to Old Trafford.

Pochettino's first game in charge will come against Liverpool at Anfield on 22nd November, just days after he is expected to lead the USMNT in their double-header against Haiti before departing, and he will have limited time to work with his new squad before having to select his team.

However, the Argentine will have around six weeks to work with the players and figure out who fits into his plans and what areas of the squad need strengthening ahead of the January transfer window.

United are expected to be in the market for a left-back and a forward at the very least following their shortcomings in the summer transfer window, and Fabrizio Romano has now revealed in his latest YouTube video that Pochettino is set to be heavily involved in the recruitmen during the winter window.

"But then in the first meetings they will have with the club, they will discuss, and that's something they already mentioned in the first contacts had between Pochettino and United," he said.

"They already said very soon we will have specific meetings regarding the January transfer window. Pochettino is a manager who loves to be involved in the transfer strategy. He's not the kind of manager who says, 'Okay, give me the players, I will take care of them.'

"Pochettino is very good also at taking care of what he has. We have to be honest; he's a coach that, especially Tottenham, showed that he can do very good things with players that are not constantly changing. But at the same time, Pochettino likes to be involved. Pochettino likes to have his say, and in this case, I expect Pochettino to be involved at Man United because they need to change.

"Already in January, they need to do something. Could be two, could be three, could be one. I don't know. We will see. But United will sign players in January. I can guarantee that because that's part of the strategy at the club, regardless of who's going to be the coach."

While new arrivals have been 'guaranteed' by Romano during the winter window, United must also start to think about making decisions on the futures of players who are already in the squad.

Harry Maguire, Luke Shaw, Lisandro Martinez, and Bruno Fernandes are all out of contract at the end of the season, with the left-back the only one who doesn't have a club option in his deal for an extra 12 months. But the chances of them all staying beyond this campaign are slim to none, and if Pochettino isn't keen on any of them, then January could be a good chance to move them on and bring in some funds.

Squad planning is an essential part of the success at football clubs, and INEOS have failed miserably so far since taking over footballing control. Pochettino's arrival, and his inclusion in talks about recruitment, are potentially a chance to get back on track, but they must be ruthless as soon as possible.

Trump says ‘we don’t need a deal with Iran’

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

The U.S. should no longer seek a deal with Iran, President Donald Trump said Thursday as he directed Vice President JD Vance to instead focus his attention on corruption and crime in Democratic cities and states.

"We don't need a deal with Iran. Those days have long since passed," Trump wrote in a Truth Social post.

"We've got their money, Hundreds of Billions of Dollars, and we've got their Strait!" Trump wrote, referring to the Strait of Hormuz, a key chokepoint in the ongoing U.S.-Iran war.

The announcement came just over a week after Republicans suffered huge losses in the midterm election. Vance and other Republicans have pinned those losses on the Iran war, which the U.S. and Israel started in late February.

In the weeks leading up to Election Day, Trump repeatedly suggested the U.S. could resume launching military strikes, which have largely subsided as a tense stalemate has taken hold in the region

The Wall Street Journal on Wednesday reported that Vance, who has led diplomatic efforts with Iran in prior months, was considering face-to-face talks with a top Iranian security official.

Stock futures declined following Trump's post.

The unpopular Iran war, which Trump and others in his administration said would be won in a matter of weeks, is now in its ninth month with no clear end in sight. The conflict region has driven up global energy prices, creating a ripple effect through the economy and exacerbating concerns about inflation and affordability that were paramount to voters in the midterms.

Corruption emerged as another top issue that broke in Democrats' favor in the election. Trump's post Thursday morning attempts to flip the script, blaming Democratic-run areas for allowing corruption and crime to proliferate.

"I have asked Vice President Vance to focus his complete and total attention on the Massive Corruption and Crime taking place in Democrat run Cities and States throughout the U.S.," Trump wrote.

"The Blue States, such as Minnesota, California, Illinois, and many more, are all poorly run, corrupt disasters. Billions of Dollars is being stolen each and every month," the president wrote. "Our Budget could be quickly BALANCED with the correction of this large scale criminal activity."

"CORRUPTION is in the Democrat Leadership's blood!" he added.

West Ham’s Leadership Problem Goes Far Beyond Championship Experience

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

West Ham’s lack of Championship experience has been highlighted by former striker Sam Parkin, who believes the Hammers are missing the know-how required to negotiate difficult away matches.

Speaking on the Sky Sports EFL Show, Parkin questioned whether West Ham have enough experienced organisers in the team, with The72 highlighting his concerns about a midfield containing Arne Engels, Mohamadou Kanté and Divine Mukasa.

Let’s be absolutely clear about this. West Ham lack leaders, full stop. I believe this is partly a consequence of Graham Potter’s clear-out, something highlighted in Michail Antonio’s book, with the former manager appearing to move on some of the bigger personalities at the club. And let’s be fair, they’ve not really been replaced.

However, I think there are two separate issues here. One is leadership, while the other is tactical nous, and I’m not convinced they’re necessarily solved by the same player.

If West Ham are looking for somebody prepared to scrap, they really need look no further than Edson Álvarez. The Mexican is already at the club despite having been frozen out under Nuno Espírito Santo, and he would immediately bring some bite to the midfield.

From a tactical point of view, I suspect Scott Parker will persevere with Soungoutou Magassa in defensive midfield once he gets back up to full fitness. Parker has already brought him back into the picture, and a specialist defensive midfielder could make a significant difference to how West Ham cope when games become physical or untidy.

Longer term, though, there is little doubt West Ham should be looking at the personalities they’re recruiting, not simply the technical ability of individual players.

When Director of Player Recruitment Nils Koppen assesses potential January signings, I’d like to think he’ll be considering whether they can organise those around them, take responsibility and provide leadership when things aren’t going well.

Parkin makes a fair point about Championship experience, but for me the issue goes deeper than knowing the division. West Ham have lost too many big personalities without replacing them, and that’s something Parker and Koppen will eventually have to address.

Kris Gonzo (commonly known to the West Ham community as Gonzo) is the co-founder of Hammers Chat (established 2014) and the lead video content creator and columnist for Claret and Hugh.

With over a decade of professional sports media experience, he is a frequent West Ham United analyst and contributor for Sky Sports News, BBC Football Focus, and BBC Radio 5 Live. He previously served as an official video and content creator for West Ham United FC during the club's tenure at Upton Park and featured in the official documentary commemorating the historic final game at the Boleyn Ground.

A lifelong Hammer since the legendary 10-0 victory over Bury, when he isn't providing daily match analysis on the Hammers Chat YouTube Channel or broadcasting on X (@GONZObignose), he can be found walking his dog and restoring classic pinball machines.

Zimbabwean author Simbarashe Kundizeza cleared of using AI to write prize-winning story

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

After a month-long investigation it was concluded that Kundizeza’s story The Blue Fighter, which won the £10,000 Caine prize, was ‘human-written’

Zimbabwean author Simbarashe Kundizeza has been cleared of using AI to write a prize-winning short story, after the prestigious Caine prize for African writers concluded that his entry was “human-written” after a month-long investigation.

Kundizeza’s story The Blue Fighter, which charts the declining fortunes of a family after the father invests in fish tanks, was announced as the winner of the £10,000 prize on 6 October.

Five days later, the Atlantic published an article titled: “Yet another literary prize winner seems to be AI,” in which author and journalist Vauhini Vara said AI-detection software Pangram, which itself uses AI, had deemed the story to be almost completely artificially generated.

Caine prize chair Ellah Wakatama said in a statement: “From our detailed investigation, we feel the evidence is clear: The Blue Fighter was human-written. We now look forward to awarding and celebrating Simbarashe Steyn Kundizeza … before the end of the year.”

She said: “When concerns were raised, we have a duty as an organisation to look at them carefully and fairly,” adding that the prize had taken a “human-centred” approach to its investigation.

“Our review included interviews with the author, the 2026 judging panel and The Shallow Tales Review, where the story was first published,” Wakatama said. “It also included independent and expert-led metadata analysis of drafts written by the author.”

Kundizeza, who had strongly denied using generative AI to write the story, said he was “relieved” that the allegations had been dismissed after the investigation. “It was not necessary in the first place, I am happy to put all this behind me and continue with my writing journey,” he said.

Vara’s article prompted outrage online, with many claiming that Black writers, and Africans in particular, were being unfairly singled out by allegations they were using AI.

Associated Press contributed to this report

Bitcoin Mining Legend Chun Wang Rebuys ETH at 14% Discount After $18.5 Million Sale

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

F2Pool co-founder Chun Wang, known on X as Satofishi, bought 5,246 ETH worth $10.78 million over the past two days at an average price of $2,054, according to Lookonchain and Arkham data.

On Nov. 5, with ether trading near $2,400, Wang sold 6,200 wrapped staked ETH (wstETH) for $18.5 million in USDC. Wrapped staked ETH is a version of Lido's staked ether (stETH) that can be used in DeFi protocols.

This week, ether traded about 14% lower than on the day of the sale, and Wang started buying it back.

He withdrew 5,246 ETH from Binance hot wallets in two transfers of 3,996 ETH and 1,250 ETH. The purchase cost him $10.78 million, so about $7.7 million from the Nov. 5 sale is still left in USDC, according to Lookonchain.

Wang then sent 3,996 ETH ($8.23 million) to Lido. Lido is the largest liquid staking protocol on Ethereum. He deposited the wstETH he received into Spark, a lending protocol in the Sky ecosystem, which was previously known as MakerDAO. In Spark, wstETH can be used as collateral, while it continues to earn staking rewards.

He started out arbitraging Bitcoin between overseas exchanges and China's Taobao and later co-founded F2Pool. He launched the pool in 2013 together with Mao Shixing. At that time, F2Pool was one of the first Bitcoin mining pools in China.

In 2015, he bought a condo in Pattaya for 2,900 BTC. He later sold it for 7 BTC, as the price of Bitcoin had risen many times over.

In 2025, Wang also funded and commanded Fram2, the first crewed spaceflight over Earth's polar regions, on a SpaceX Crew Dragon.

F2Pool also mined ether until Ethereum moved to proof-of-stake in September 2022. Before that, Wang launched the non-custodial staking service stakefish. According to Wang, he decided to do it after a conversation with Vitalik Buterin in 2017. Stakefish was also one of the first node operators of Lido.

Ether is down more than 15% since the start of the month amid outflows from spot ETFs, according to SoSoValue. At press time, ETH is trading at $2,020.

Learn how to submit your website to ChatGPT through Business Hub, verify your business, and understand what claiming changes for SEO and brand visibility.

OpenAI introduced the long-awaited Businesses in ChatGPT feature in beta on November 10, 2026. You can submit your website’s business information through Business Hub to claim or create a profile and provide details about your company.

I’m excited about this, and I’m sure other digital marketers, not just SEOs, are too and after submitting Search Engine Journal to ChatGPT, I decided to share my firsthand experience with SEJ’s readers.

If you choose the physical-location option to create a new business, you may see a waitlist. That happened in my test. The website option let me continue with Search Engine Journal.

Existing local listings have a different process. OpenAI’s setup guide says stores and restaurants can currently claim an existing U.S. listing.

For those listings, select Business phone if it is offered, then choose SMS or an automated call to receive a code. Make sure the listed phone number is reachable. If you choose a call, do it during business hours when someone can answer and an auto-responder won’t prevent the verification call from reaching you.

Unlike Google Business Profile, ChatGPT Business Hub accepts eligible online-only businesses. Google lists “Brands, organizations, artists, and other online-only businesses” under “Ineligible businesses” in its eligibility guidelines. For SEJ, this meant I could submit the publication as a website business.

Whether submitting a profile brings more visibility is another question. OpenAI says claiming a business does not guarantee higher placement. Its business overview explains that ChatGPT still makes its own recommendations and comparisons.

In this guide, we will walk through the submission process step by step and look at how to read the insights in Business Hub.

Before you start, make sure you have access to your domain’s DNS settings and your business records. I had to verify the website domain and my identity, then provide the business details.

For SEJ, I entered Search Engine Journal as the business name and used its DBA details. DBA stands for “doing business as.” I selected Entity as the business type.

One field caused some confusion. It asked for a registration number, but the 12-1234567 placeholder looked like a U.S. Employer Identification Number, or EIN. A state registration number is different, so check which identifier the form requests for your country before entering it.

Sign in to ChatGPT, open Business Hub, and search for your business name. Select the matching business if it appears. If there is no match, look for the option to create a new profile.

In a separate physical-location test, creating a new business profile led to a waitlist. ChatGPT said it would send an email when I could continue.

For SEJ, I selected Through a website because readers access the publication online. This took me to domain verification.

ChatGPT asked me to verify SEJ’s domain by adding a TXT record to its DNS settings.

Copy ChatGPT’s TXT record type, name, and value into your domain’s DNS settings. After the update takes effect, return to ChatGPT to verify the domain. See OpenAI’s verification instructions.

This confirmed control of the domain. I still had to verify my identity and submit the business details later in the process.

Once I verified the domain, ChatGPT asked me to choose a category from a predefined list and add an optional description.

I selected Magazine publisher for SEJ. ChatGPT also suggested Online media company and News service.

The description field said ChatGPT could share these details in relevant conversations. For a publisher, this is a place to explain the topics it covers and who it writes for.

I would expect ChatGPT to use this context when matching your services with relevant questions. Write the description with SEO and GEO in mind, using the language your customers use to describe what they need.

Next, I could add a business email address and phone number. Both fields were optional.

ChatGPT then asked about delivery and returns. Those questions felt out of place for SEJ, especially after I had selected a publishing category.

The delivery choices covered no delivery, paid delivery, and free delivery. The following screen asked whether returns were unavailable, accepted with fees, or free.

These choices appear in the profile preview. Check how they describe your business before continuing, especially if the questions don’t fit what you do.

ChatGPT then saved SEJ’s profile as a draft and directed me to the business overview to continue verification or check its status.

The same screen also offered $500 in advertising credit and an upgrade to a ChatGPT Business plan. The credit has eligibility and redemption terms, so check those before planning to use it.

You can manage an approved profile without running ads or publishing a Business Agent, according to OpenAI’s setup FAQ.

As director of technology at Search Engine Journal, I submitted SEJ’s information.

OpenAI’s eligibility requirement allows either the owner or an authorized representative to manage a business profile.

I completed the personal identity check first, then entered SEJ’s business information.

Review these details before submitting, because approval automatically publishes new profiles created through Persona. Approved Persona verification also adds the blue badge, according to OpenAI’s Persona guidance.

For SEJ, verification was almost instant. I captured the review screen below before approval.

If your verification is pending, return to the business overview to check its status.

To see how often ChatGPT references your business, Business Hub provides mentions and website clicks. OpenAI’s launch announcement says these insights use aggregated data with reporting thresholds. Businesses do not receive people’s private conversations.

A mention has a specific meaning here. For an online business, ChatGPT must reference its website. Mentioning the brand name alone does not count, according to OpenAI’s insights definition.

SEJ’s mentions chart below spans October 14 to November 10, 2026. The view shows 353,356 mentions and 2,663 website clicks, with data through November 10 in UTC. Daily mentions increased by roughly 90% between the first and last day, based on the chart.

These figures become more useful alongside your website analytics. Compare the same date range to see whether changes in mentions came with changes in traffic or conversions.

Business Hub also lets you give teammates access to manage the profile. From the business overview, select Invite in the upper-right corner. The Invite business admins dialog lets you add their email address.

OpenAI’s access guidance says the admin who claimed the business can invite teammates and grant an agency access. Each person should use their own ChatGPT account. If the profile was claimed through a ChatGPT workspace, invitees may need to be members of that workspace.

This is similar to Google Business Profile’s owner and manager access. Both let several people help maintain a business profile using their own accounts. The person who submits the business does not have to handle every update.

Business Hub gives you control over the information you submit about your company. Whether that leads to more recommendations is something we still need to measure.

For a brand with inconsistent profiles, I would start by submitting the current company name and description in Business Hub. I would also check its name, address, and phone number (called NAP) across its website, business listings, and social profiles. Keep them accurate and consistent for the same business or location.

Our guide identifies local citations as a ranking factor for Google’s local search results. That consistency still matters for AI systems, which may use several sources to describe the same business. Updating Business Hub is one part of that work. I would also correct outdated details wherever they appear.

If a rebrand has left both the old and new names in circulation, explain that relationship on your site and in the business description where relevant. Keep the legal name in the verification fields that request it.

This is why I would treat Business Hub as one source of business information, rather than assume it overrides everything else ChatGPT finds. OpenAI’s profile policy says profiles can include information from other sources.

If your company owns several brands, each needs to be managed individually. Business Hub does not yet offer a central way to manage a parent company’s brands and locations. Claiming the main brand does not extend that claim to its locations, according to OpenAI’s guidance on managing brands and locations.

For brands going through a managed claim process, access to individual location profiles is restricted by default. Ask your OpenAI account contact which claiming process to follow for those locations.

Business Hub is still new, and there is more to explore beyond submitting a profile. If you sell products, the next step could be adding a feed with pricing and details using OpenAI’s file-upload guide. Agent builder is another feature to explore as it gradually rolls out to more regions. I expect these features to improve over time and OpenAI to eventually launch a Search Console-style tool for ChatGPT.

Accurate business information and websites that AI systems can access matter, and Business Hub now helps us understand how AI platforms describe our brands and send us traffic.

Muscle Memory: Heathrow Tail Strike Wrote Off 33-Year-Old Airbus A300 After Aborted Landing

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Just over a year ago, an Airbus A300-600F cargo aircraft operated by European Air Transport Leipzig as part of Deutsche Post's DHL-branded airfreight operations experienced a dramatic tail strike upon landing at London Heathrow Airport (LHR). The jet subsequently went around and landed at the second time of asking, but the damage had been done, and it only flew once more before being written off.

Now, a bulletin issued by the Air Accidents Investigation Branch of the United Kingdom has explored the finer details of the incident, ultimately finding that muscle memory had resulted in reverse thrust being selected despite the decision to go around. While the aircraft was able to be repaired temporarily so that it could be flown into storage, the cost of fully bringing it back up to speed was just too high.

As detailed by the AAIB bulletin, the incident occurred on October 12, 2025, with the A300 in question bearing the registration D-AEAG. According to Planespotters.net, this aircraft was 33 years old at the time, having first flown passengers in Japan in 1992. Its conversion to a cargo jet took place in 2011. Data from Flightradar24 shows that the flight in question was QY2212 from Leipzig Halle Airport (LEJ).

The aircraft had two pilots on board, and was cleared to land at 5:12 PM local time (4:12 PM UTC), having left Leipzig around an hour and a half earlier. As seen in the video embedded above, the aircraft's first landing attempt was captured on a livestream, with the jet bouncing as it touched down. This prompted the pilot to initiate a go-around, but, as the jet climbed away, it suffered a tail strike. The AAIB explains:

"The commander increased the aircraft’s pitch, which, combined with the continued extension of the ground spoilers and their associated pitch-up effect, resulted in a tailstrike."

Now, more than a year later, the AAIB's bulletin reveals the finer details that contributed to the tail strike and the subsequent aircraft write-off. The AAIB's findings show that muscle memory seemingly kicked in, with the pilot having selected reverse thrust. While this is a key component of a standard landing, they did so without conscious acknowledgement, meaning that their co-pilot was unable to correct them.

As such, this complicated the go-around procedure, with the engines being slower to respond once the pilot opted to go-around and selected takeoff/go-around thrust. This resulted in a drop in speed, and, while the jet did eventually take off again, it took several seconds to do so, compounding the tail strike caused by the pitch-up effect that came about as a result of the aforementioned ground spoilers.

The aircraft subsequently landed safely at the second time of asking, but the damage had been done, with the tail strike causing "damage to the lower tail section at frame 77." This, the AAIB says, "included buckling of the skin inwards at the frame, the rivets joining the skin to the frame having separated from the skin, and the tail skid having sustained abrasion damage." It ultimately proved too costly to repair.

Following its dramatic arrival in London, D-AEAG remained on the ground at Heathrow Airport for more than three months. While the resulting damage was too expensive to repair to the standard that would allow the jet to re-enter commercial service, it was patched up for one final flight, as seen above. This occurred on January 31, as D-AEAG flew from London to Lleida (ILD) for storage and scrapping.

Found an error? Send it info@simpleflying.com so it can be corrected.

The nature of the temporary repairs meant that the aircraft could only cruise at an altitude of 10,000 feet. This is often the case for post-repair ferry flights where pressurization may be an issue. It took off from London at 8:53 AM and landed in Lleida at 12:14 PM on January 31, just over two hours later. This brought an early end to its flying career, which ended, as many do, at a Spanish aircraft storage facility.

Turn Your Pool Workout Into Playtime With These Game-Inspired Exercises

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

Turn your swim workouts into games with these fun ideas for making the pool more enjoyable. (Photo: Challenge Family)

We know all the excuses to skip a swim because we’ve used many of them ourselves. Either it’s too cold outside or it’s too beautiful a day to pass up a bike ride. Or perhaps there’s a storm 100 miles away that might suddenly decide to blow right over the aquatics center. But the truth is, if your workout is interesting enough, you’ll have all the excuses you need to get yourself into the pool, not out of it.

And we’re not just talking about adding in a random drill here and there. Gamifying your swim workout, whether through solo exercises or team activities, helps make a workout enticing enough that you’ll want to do it over and over again. Adding an element of play to your swim can make it more challenging, productive, and fun all at once.

Before we get into a handful of our favorite games and drills, we asked Sage Hopkins, head coach of Women’s Swimming & Diving at San José State University, for his tips on how to make swimming more enjoyable. With these tools in your arsenal, you might not skip your pool workouts anymore, but actually look forward to them instead.

First, Hopkins stresses how crucial it is to hear laughter echoing across the pool deck. This might not be possible if you’re training alone, but if you have a training buddy who always keeps you smiling, make it a priority to meet with them more regularly. Hopkins also encourages using music to establish an upbeat atmosphere; that might look like playing a pump-up song on your way to the pool, or investing in a pair of swimming headphones to keep the tunes going lap after lap.

Once you know your purpose, Hopkins says, it’s easier to figure out what kinds of activities will make swimming more meaningful and enjoyable. Those who enjoy the camaraderie aspect of swimming and triathlon will enjoy team games like sharks and minnows.

Even simple details like naming a set, Hopkins says, can add a thrill to swimming. If there’s a test set you’re always dreading, give it a quirky or amusing name to make it seem less intimidating. You could use the most challenging triathlon you’ve ever lined up for as inspiration (The Wales One Hundreds, for instance, or the Norseman No Breathers).

Now, without further ado, let the games begin!

You don’t need any fancy clubs to play a round of swim golf — just enough glycogen in your head to add numbers. Hopkins suggests this drill for data-driven triathletes because it’s easy to keep track of your progression over time. Essentially, you add the number of strokes with the number of seconds that it takes you to complete a certain distance (such as 50 yards) to get a “score.” You’ll then repeat that distance several times within a single set, trying to keep your score as low as possible. This game helps improve your distance per stroke and ultimately conserve energy.

Healthy suspense invites bursts of dopamine and curiosity into your workout, and it also means that there’s no one to blame but fate for back-to-back sprint sets. Make sure to write the following rules down on a whiteboard or protected piece of paper to reduce confusion.

Start by grabbing a set of waterproof playing cards, and assign a technique to each suit; for example, spades might call for kicking, hearts for drills, diamonds for swimming, and clubs for pulling. Next, assign a distance to groups of numbers (for instance, 2s and 3s might mean 50s, while 4s and 5s might mean 100s, and so on). Keep your jacks, queens, kings, and aces in a separate pile because they’ll dictate the number of repeats (1, 2, 3, 4, and 5, respectively).

So, for example, pulling a 5 of clubs alongside a queen would require you to grab your pull buoy for 2×100. Pulling a 2 of hearts alongside an ace would mean a 5×50 drill.

Another way to bring a measure of unpredictability to your workout is to roll a single die four times to determine your next set. The first roll determines your technique (5s could require kicking), the second your effort level (perhaps 4s always mean sprints), the third your distance (1s could mean 50s), and the fourth your number of reps (anywhere from 1 to 6).

If you were on a summer swim team as a kid, chances are high you played many games of sharks and minnows. The game starts out with one “shark” who tries to tag all the other “minnows” as they swim from one side of the pool to the other. Each swimmer who gets tagged turns into a shark for the next round. You can complicate this game by instituting an underwater safety zone.

If you have an area of the pool to yourself, form teams, set up goals on opposite walls with pull buoys, grab a ball, and play water polo. This is an excellent way to make vertical kicking, sprinting, and treading water more exciting. It also facilitates team bonding.

Head to the deep end for this game to work on endurance as you tread water and practice vertical kicking. If the lifeguards allow it, you can play water volleyball over the flags with an inflatable beach ball. Your pool might even have a water volleyball net, and some versions float so that you can move them as required.

Hopkins recommends “call it sets,” inspired by Bob Steele, to work on pacing and fine-tuning your internal clock. Essentially, you and your training partners will swim a set of 100s, and before each repeat, each one of you will predict the time you expect to hit. Whoever is closest to their estimates wins the set.

Chase sets are a fun way for training groups with seemingly disparate ability levels to unite, says Hopkins. Slower swimmers are allowed a head start, while faster swimmers are tasked with trying to catch them. Chase sets are ideally employed for speedwork, not drills.

Casino night is extraordinarily fun for large teams and helps highlight everyone’s unique talents. First, two or three swimmers who are roughly equivalent in speed line up at the blocks. Then, the other teammates line up behind the swimmer they predict will win.

From there, you’ll use cards or dice to determine the “event” that the swimmers will be competing in (see general rules above in the solo workouts). Whoever has picked the winning swimmer gets a rest break while the rest of the team completes a penalty swim (a 50 sprint, perhaps). If you have one seemingly unbeatable swimmer on the team, you can assign them a permanent handicap.

Sunderland will be relegated if they lose Brian Brobbey to injury - he is Black Cats' cheat code and most influential player

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Sunderland centre-forward Brian Brobbey has made himself indispensable at the Stadium of Light

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Granit Xhaka brings European pedigree and composure in the middle of the park, Enzo Le Fee provides creative thrust, whilst Robin Roefs has proven to be a Premier League-standard goalkeeper between the posts - but none of them are Sunderland's most important player.

It is easy to understand why there may be debate among Sunderland supporters over who is club’s most vital asset. But looking plainly at what is keeping Sunderland afloat in the Premier League, the reality is obvious: it's Brian Brobbey.

A telling data visualisation published by X/Twitter user markstats.club highlights just how unprecedented Brobbey’s contribution to the Black Cats' cause is across the English top flight.

The graphic plots Premier League forwards' ability at contesting long balls during the 2026/27 campaign, measuring how often an attacker is first to the ball per 90 minutes, against the percentage of those duels that result in their team retaining possession.

Under normal circumstances, there is an inevitable trade-off for centre-forwards. Strikers who contest a high number of long balls, such as Leeds United's Dominic Calvert-Lewin (7.1 per 90) and Nottingham Forest's Liam Delap (6.6 per 90), invariably lose their fair share.

In these two case studies, Leeds and Forest retain the ball just 53% and 38% of the time, respectively. Strikers Erling Haaland and Taiwo Awoniyi boast high retention rates between 67% and 70%, but contest far fewer long balls (both under 3.5 per 90).

For the most part it's very much one or the other: high volume OR high retention, not both.

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

Brobbey is the only player in the Premier League this season who bucks that trend.

The Dutchman is contesting nearly five long balls per 90 minutes (4.9), matching the workload of the division's workhorses, but Sunderland retain the ball on an astonishing 79% of those occasions.

He is the league's single-most radical outlier - nobody else in the Premier League can boast this combination of physical volume and security.

Brian Brobbey is the best target man in the Premier League this season When Sunderland go long from their own half and he gets to the ball first, they keep it 79% of the time. The average for forwards is 53%. Calvert-Lewin, who wins far more long balls, is at 53%, Delap at 38% https://t.co/Q5Ftm51HOxNovember 8, 2026

The broader tactical implications for Sunderland’s survival fight must not be overstated, though. For a relatively newly established top-flight side often forced into a low or mid-block, navigating sustained opposition pressure is simply a fact of Premier League life.

Without a forward capable of absorbing contact, killing dead balls and providing relief, clearances turn into recurring waves of defending.

Brobbey, then, functions as Sunderland's release valve. Whether it be pinning a defender, taking a 50-yard diagonal on his chest, or using his lower-body power to spin his marker, as he did so effectively against Chelsea defender Levi Colwill for the Black Cats' penalty last weekend, he transforms defensive clearances into controlled attacking-third possession.

That security allows Sunderland’s defensive line to step up, resets the team's shape, and gives orchestrators like Xhaka and Le Fee a platform to operate 40 yards further up the pitch without fear of immediate turnovers.

Losing Xhaka would harm Sunderland’s tactical discipline, losing Le Fee would diminish their incisiveness in the final third, and losing Roefs would likely lead to a drop-off in shot-stopping. But, losing Brobbey would cut right at the heart of how the team functions for large parts of games.

Without his ability to turn long balls into Sunderland possession, Regis Le Bris' side would either be forced to play through pressing traps they might not survive or watch clearances bounce straight back into their defensive third.

If Sunderland keep Brobbey fit, his hold-up play will keep them competitive against anyone. If he spends an extended period on the treatment table, the trapdoor will open, which given their first 10 games of the season, they can ill afford.

Blackmagic’s $1,695 Spatial Audio Mic Has 108 Mics and Looks Super Sci-Fi

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Blackmagic’s $1,695 Spatial Audio Mic Has 108 Mics and Looks Super Sci-Fi

Blackmagic Design has announced the Ambisonic Microphone, a new professional mic designed to capture spatial audio for immersive video productions.

“The Blackmagic Ambisonic Microphone is a 7th order ambisonic microphone array featuring 108 digital MEMS microphones that capture the entire sound field,” Blackmagic explains. “The MEMS microphones are mounted flush in a solid, faceted metal body, making the design extremely strong for professional use while remaining small enough to fit in the palm of your hand, and be mounted on top of a camera.”

Advanced audio processing technology is built directly into the microphone, and an internal processor runs Apple’s encoding filters, converting the 108 MEMS microphones to seventh-order ambisonics in real time. This means customers don’t need complicated software or a breakout box.

The mic is powered by a single USB-C cable, and it can quickly be mounted to an URSA Cine Immersive camera. Capturing spatial audio is as simple as plugging it in.

As Blackmagic Design explains, its new microphone started development when it was working with Apple on the Blackmagic URSA Cine Immersive camera, which was formally announced in June 2024 and billed as “the ultimate camera system for making immersive video for the Apple Vision Pro.”

Filmmakers have used the Blackmagic URSA Cine Immersive to capture all sorts of incredible things, including basketball games and rocket launches.

“This microphone started when we were collaborating with Apple on the Blackmagic URSA Cine Immersive camera. I heard a high resolution spatial audio capture for the first time and it was like hearing an acoustic reality, I was totally blown away,” says Blackmagic Design CEO Grant Petty.

“Apple licensed their array geometry and the ambisonic encoding filters, we did the engineering and design, and the result is an ambisonic microphone where you just plug in one cable and capture true spatial audio.”

The Blackmagic Ambisonic Microphone will be available next month for $1,695 through Blackmagic Design and its authorized retailers.

10 Things I’m Dreading About Thru-Hiking the Appalachian Trail

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

I recently wrote about the 10 things I’m most excited about when it comes to thru-hiking the Appalachian Trail.

However, as any Appalachian Trail thru-hiker can tell you, the AT is a trail that throws a lot at you. As excited as I am to experience such an iconic trail culture, I am dreading some of the more uncomfortable aspects of the journey. So, before I willingly sign up for five months of relentless discomfort, here’s what I am dreading most about thru-hiking the Appalachian Trail.

Coming from the dry air of the West, the sheer humidity of the East Coast is a fear of mine. I know what it’s like to sweat, but I’m used to everything drying out overnight. There is a special kind of misery in waking up damp, packing away a wet tent, and immediately sweating through my clothes before I even start the first climb.

Drying gear out feels nearly impossible when the air itself feels wet, and I know I need to accept that I will seldom feel truly dry on this trail. That’s easy to write about from my comfortable couch; yet in practice, I fear that the persistent dampness will chip away at my mental fortitude more than I can currently anticipate.

Rain gear can only do so much. Photo via Katie Jackson

While the shade and lightning protection are comforting, I find the prospect of the AT’s endless Green Tunnel daunting. On other trails I’ve hiked, even the tree-covered sections would frequently open onto a high ridge or exposed pass that rewarded me with fantastic views. The AT’s canopy shelters hikers from their hard-earned views more than I’m used to, and I once again fear the mental toll of not seeing the vistas I love.

I know the answer is to learn to love the minute changes of the Green Tunnel, but that’s a big ask when I’m accustomed to walking through some of the grandest, most objectively stunning views in America. Will I be able to find a deeper motivation for a thru-hike other than seeing pretty places? Your guess is as good as mine.

The trees are beautiful, but after a while I want to see some MOUNTAINS! Photo via Katie Jackson

Ticks freak me out in a way I struggle to articulate. As someone with a deep fear of needles, something about looking down and seeing a bug with its head buried inside your skin makes the back of my neck itch. Even the concept of tick checks causes some anxiety; the possibility of finding a tick on me is as stressful as the creature itself.

To make matters worse, this isn’t a fear I can just tamp down and avoid. The consequences of leaving a tick attached can be severe. Lyme disease is on the rise in ticks found out East, along with a whole host of other tick-borne illnesses. The idea of having to perform a full-body tick check every single night adds a persistent, low-grade anxiety to camp life that I’ve never experienced on a trail before.

I’ll also throw snakes on this list. I’m not stoked by the number of venomous snakes out there. Photo via Katie Jackson

Western trails can be steep, technical, exposed, and rocky, but the long-distance trails are often graded with switchbacks and are designed for foot traffic. The AT, by contrast, frequently takes the most direct route up and down the mountains’ fall lines, and much of the trail is naturally composed of tree roots, mud pits, and slick rock.

My pace while hiking the Long Trail was slower than any long trail I’ve done before; to keep myself from falling on the challenging terrain, I sometimes had to slow down to nearly a crawl. This unpredictable terrain stresses my joints and muscles in a way that a clear singletrack doesn’t. I’ll be going into this trail with a persistent back injury, so I’m worried that five months of awkward steps will be a bit too much for my body to handle.

Not only is it very humid, but the trail itself doesn’t make it easy to stay dry. Photo via Katie Jackson

Even in the best of circumstances, I don’t sleep well on trails (or in normal life). One thing I do love about sleeping on thru-hikes is the total silence that comes with the backcountry. However, on the AT, I don’t expect to spend much time at all in the deep backcountry.

While the noise of nearby roads or towns isn’t the end of the world, I truly dread sharing shelters with the worst wildlife of all: snorers.

I know that hiker etiquette suggests choosing a different spot to sleep if you know yourself to be a snorer, but I’m also not naive enough to expect all hikers to follow that social contract. People suggest earplugs, but I’ve never found earplugs to actually block noise for me. Maybe I have oddly shaped ear canals, maybe I’m hypersensitive to the sound, or maybe I just haven’t found the right pair quite yet.

Either way, I worry that I will end up not able to utilize the convenience of the shelters because staying in one with a snorer will lead to a sleepless night.

Shelters seem so comfortable and convenient, but I know I need to prioritize being able to actually fall asleep. Photo via Katie Jackson

Hiking southbound means you are racing the calendar from day one, and as the weeks tick by, watching the daylight shrink is a constant stress. Out West, even when days get shorter in the fall, you usually have wide-open skies that let you stretch your hiking hours into the late dusk. Under the heavy canopy of the AT, once the sun starts dropping, the Green Tunnel goes pitch black in a hurry.

I’m not opposed to night hiking, but I don’t have a ton of experience with it. In order to meet the pace I’m hoping to keep on trail, I know I’ll have to get a lot more accustomed to it. That will either turn into a pleasant surprise as I learn I really love hiking after dark, or it’ll be a constant frustration.

The woods after dark can get a little spooky. Photo via Katie Jackson

While I love a trail town, the AT in particular seems like a trail that could suck me into a town and make it difficult to leave. Towns, I’ve found, are the single biggest cause of my spending on thru-hikes, so I want to try my best to avoid financial traps when possible. Hostels are unfortunately too comfortable and I love a meal from a restaurant too much to resist when I’m near one; and, on the AT, you’re always near one.

I don’t personally think that spending time in towns will affect my momentum or motivation, but I have a very specific amount of money saved for this hike and I fear for my budget. On the CDT, I spent less than $5,000 on my hike, largely by hitting the trail already in shape and minimizing my time in towns. On the AT, I worry that neither of these things will be possible for me.

I can’t help it! I love a side quest! Photo via Katie Jackson

I have never felt grosser than I did at the Northern Terminus of the CDT. Like many, I struggle to eat while hiking, especially if I’m trying to force down food I’m not thrilled about. Combined with the food desert that blankets much of that trail, I found myself eating pure junk for five months straight. By the end of the trail, my diet of Honey Buns and sticks of butter left me exhausted, poorly recovered, and craving a vegetable.

This is more of a general worry about thru-hiking than an AT-specific fear. This time around, I really want to force myself to eat better, but I worry I’ll run into the same problem with a lack of appetite. I’m not sure I can mentally or physically handle five more months of Little Debbie’s products, but I also know my body will need every calorie I can get my hands on.

I know I’m a generally stressed out person. Photo via Katie Jackson

While part of my affinity for cowboy camping stems from my laziness about setting up my tent, I truly love opening my eyes at night and seeing the wide expanse of stars in the sky with the Milky Way slashing across.

For so many reasons (bugs and rain), I know that frequent cowboy camping on the AT is generally a little less realistic. Even if I do choose to sleep out under the stars, the odds that I actually get a clear view of the sky through the trees feel low.

Even in the daytime, I’m massively attached to the wide-open sky sprawl of the West. I love seeing the weather roll in hours in advance. I love seeing glimpses of the trail miles away. Trading those sweeping views for something that feels a bit more sheltered and claustrophobic already makes me a little bit sad.

I love an open, expansive view. Photo via Katie Jackson

Opting to start southbound means beginning my hike in the relatively unforgiving Maine terrain. There is no gentle “ease in” period to build trail legs, such as what I had in New Mexico on the CDT. I’ll be thrown directly into some of the steepest and most technical terrain on the entire trail.

In my experience on the CDT, I felt physically the strongest near the middle but actually felt weaker and weaker as I hiked further into Montana and the hike started to wear on my body. Therefore, I’m trying to remind myself that Maine may be almost equally as challenging for me if I were to go NOBO; if my brain and body are exhausted from the culmination of the hike, I still won’t be in great shape to take on the climbs, even if I feel like I should be.

Still, knowing that the first week on trail will likely be a masterclass in physical suffering is a terrifying way to start a long journey.

I’ve done enough hiking in the White Mountains to know it won’t really get any easier after Maine. Photo via Katie Jackson

Pre-hike nerves are expected before a trail, or at least that’s what I keep telling myself.

I’ve loved every long trail I’ve had the privilege of hiking before, even the ones I didn’t expect to deeply love, so I’m excited to face these fears, figure out which ones don’t actually bother me at all, and work through the remainder while also discovering all the surprises about the trail that I love.

I'm Katie, a thru-hiker and ultrarunner based in Colorado. After thru-hiking the Colorado Trail, John Muir Trail, Continental Divide Trail, and Long Trail in back-to-back years, I'm looking forward to a little time of rest, relaxation, and hanging out with my cat. Or, who knows, maybe the AT. No one has ever accused me of being good at sitting still.

Morning Minute: Tokenized Stock Venues Are Launching Without Big Tech’s Approval

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Morning Minute: Tokenized Stock Venues Are Launching Without Big Tech’s Approval

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

🏛️ OKX’s Tokenized Stock Venue Launches Without Big Tech

In September, after the CLARITY Act died in the Senate, the SEC created an innovation exemption letting venues trade tokenized versions of US stocks without waiting for new legislation. It came with one condition. A company that doesn’t want a third party minting tokens of its shares can object in writing within 30 days, and that’s the end of it.

Companies have been objecting ever since. The count at the OKXICE venue went from 22 in October to 50, then 56, and as of its November 5 notice, 67. Every member of the Magnificent Seven is on that list.

OKXICE is launching anyway, on November 23. The venue will list more than 70 assets, though 59 of them are Direxion ETFs. The individual stocks that made it through are Coinbase, Circle, Robinhood, Strategy, BitGo, Securitize and Better.com. Strategy is also tokenizing its preferred shares.

Securitize handles the tokenization, each token is backed one-for-one by a real share and carries dividends and voting rights, and trading runs through Uniswap v4 pools on X Layer, OKX’s Ethereum layer-2.

So a venture between a global exchange and the parent of the New York Stock Exchange built a tokenized equities market, and what it opens with is seven crypto companies and a series of leveraged ETFs.

Notably, this news comes just a day after the NYSE picked Avalanche to handle clearing and settlement for its own 24/7 tokenized securities platform, with a fourth-quarter target for launch after about a year of testing. The NYSE has signed agreements with Broadridge, Computershare, Equiniti and Superstate to support issuer-sponsored tokenized securities and transfer-agent functions. When the company’s own share registry issues the token, there is nothing to veto.

DTCC is doing a version of this too, with a tokenization service that went live October 28 on the Canton Network carrying 20 participants including Bank of America, Citi, JPMorgan and Citadel Securities. Morgan Stanley has already run equities and Treasuries through it.

Progress is being made, slowly but surely. It just gets built slowly, through transfer agents and clearing houses, by the incumbents. Now we wait to see how the market reacts to these first launches…
